# Changelog

secureconfig is published continuously and versions as `1.0.<pull request number>`, where the number is
that of the most recently merged pull request. The current value is in the `VERSION` file at the
repository root. There is no release artifact, so the version names a state of `main` rather than a
downloadable build, and each guide remains dated by its own "Sources (checked <month year>)" section.
Entries here are grouped by the UTC date the change landed on `main`. That rule applies from 2026-09-27;
earlier headings were not held to one timezone and are left as recorded. Note that nothing in the gate
suite can check `VERSION` against GitHub: no gate reaches the network, by design, so that no outside
service can change a gate's answer, and a pull request number is only knowable from outside. Keeping
`VERSION` in step with the merged pull request is therefore an authoring obligation, not an enforced one.

As binary reading of the index Docker Hub served on 2026-10-24 (`sha256:73aaf090f3d85aa34ee199857f03fa3a95c8ede2ffd4cc2cdb5b94e566b11662`), with nothing run, each of its nine linux images has a BusyBox v1.36.1 binary, linked from `bin/nc`, that carries the 1.10-compatible nc option string `^np:s:ubvw:+lki:o:z` and not the small nc's `lp:w:i:f:e:`. The index's amd64 entry names docker-library/busybox revision c1375496 and version 1.36.1-glibc, which agrees with the guide's mapping without proving the build link, and the guide keeps its advice to confirm `nc -z -w 3` and a TCP connect at run time. - Records: the lychee `--dump` truncation check under TODO 3.40 is recorded as a partial DONE 3.40 row. In a lychee 0.24.2 `--dump` run by the orchestrator over e9255153 (offline extraction, no link checked), this change's read-only assessment found every dated Sources URL (5,931 occurrences) and every claims component source URL (5,685) dumped whole, and the fenced claims JSON not extracted. The pack records that this run lacked e9255153's `--max-retries 1`, and that a re-run with every e9255153 argument except `--accept` printed the same lines in another order. - Weekly link check: `.github/workflows/linkcheck.yml` now passes `--max-retries 1` to lychee v0.24.2 instead of the default three retries, and raises the job's `timeout-minutes` from 45 to 75. The 2026-10-19 scheduled sweep (run 39209362953) was cancelled at the 45-minute job timeout while `git.savannah.gnu.org/cgit` requests were timing out. By source reading, not shown directly in the log, lychee v0.24.2 sends requests for one URL one at a time whatever the fragment and does not share a timeout along that queue, so each retry lengthened it. Two attempts at the full 20-second request timeout for each of the 80 occurrences of one URL are estimated at about 53 minutes, or about 57 with the per-attempt delay and the 1.6 minutes before the first savannah result seen in the log, which 45 minutes would not fit; a larger limit only lets more results complete, and a sweep that still exceeds it fails closed. Only timeouts and some network errors are retried here, never a 404 or 410, and the dead-links-only rule and the fail-closed handling of an incomplete sweep are unchanged. Whether a sweep now completes in time is shown only by the next scheduled run (2026-10-26) or a manual dispatch. TODO 3.40 stays open: its sweep confirmation is owed on the first completed sweep after this change. (#841)

- Closed TODO 1.334 in `observability-components.md`: by source reading of Loki v3.7.8's push validation, without a Loki run, an ingester can hold an entry timestamped exactly at the future start of the inverted ingester interval that the querier sends for a negative store lookback other than `-1` when now minus it is after the selection end. On the future side, the distributor rejects only timestamps strictly newer than now plus the tenant's `creation_grace_period` (default 10m), so one exactly at that bound passes, though with `increment_duplicate_timestamp` on (default false) it sets, after validation, each different-line duplicate after the first to the previous stored entry's timestamp plus one nanosecond, so a push of n such entries at the bound can store the last one n-1 nanoseconds past it; the ingester's own push chain bounds entries against stream state and schema configuration, never the clock (ordered appends reject against the head block's newest or, with the head block empty, the previous block's maximum), so it imposes no clock-relative upper bound on a stored entry's timestamp, and the Kafka partition-ingester consumer pushes into the same ingester push code; in the guide's assessment the interval's start lies at or before the distributor's latest acceptable timestamp whenever the negative lookback's magnitude is at most the grace period, assuming one reference clock and an unchanged grace period. Added `loki-push-future-bound`, `loki-ingester-push-validation` and `loki-ingester-inverted-hold` with 23 new source keys, dropped the untraced-holding clause from `loki-ingester-inverted-time-filter`, and revised `loki-querier-ingester-inverted-result` so only the unobserved runtime result, with the Kafka consumer and partition-ingester paths, stays open under 1.301. (#806)

- `observability-components.md` (TODO 1.301, partial): as a source reading at Loki v3.7.8 without a run, the inverted ingester interval's return path is now traced. The ingesters' index lookup picks a per-period index by the interval's start and matches without reading time, so the interval's order causes no lookup error. No stream, chunk or head-block iterator swaps or clamps the interval, and the chunk time-range filters pass only entries dated exactly at its future start (this guide's assessment). The unordered head block queries its vendored go-datastructures v1.1.7 range tree from the start to one nanosecond before the end, both inclusive, so the query returns no entry for the interval and backward log queries get nothing from it. An iterator that yields nothing becomes one empty ingester batch, sent unless the request context is already done and raising no error unless that send returns an error other than `context.Canceled`; the ingester querier yields it as nothing and the querier merges that with any store query it makes. Within Loki's own `pkg`, `cmd`, `integration`, `tools` and `clients` trees, nothing sets `Ingester.Wrapper`. Runtime results and externally set wrappers stay open, and whether ingesters can hold an entry at that exact start is a new row. (#802)
- Claim lengths are now gated (TODO 3.44, closed; maintainer ruling 2026-10-23, P6 option C): `tools/check_claim_lengths.py` refuses any enrolled claim whose `text` exceeds 400 characters, counted in Unicode code points through `version_basis.split()`, unless `tools/claim_length_baseline.txt` lists it at exactly its current length: the gate refuses an over-length claim with no entry, a claim whose length differs from its entry, and stale entries; a malformed or unsorted baseline, a baseline line carrying a control or line-separator character other than tab, and an entry naming an unenrolled guide, a missing claim or a length at or under 400 fail closed. Adding an entry or raising a length is a baseline edit that only review can refuse, because the gate reads the tree only. The baseline is seeded with the 104 over-length claims in 32 of the 100 enrolled guides measured at this checkout, the figure recorded at 349a642b, and `.gitattributes` keeps it LF in every checkout. `tools/test_claim_lengths.py` carries 53 recorded cases, including a CRLF-saved baseline refused line by line and the disclosed tree-only limit, and both run in `tools/run_all_checks.sh`. P6 moves out of PENDING-DECISIONS.md, DECISIONS.md records the ruling, CONTRIBUTING.md names the limit and the baseline, and TODO row 1.326 now points at the gate and its baseline. (#801)

## 2026-10-22

- `pocketbase.md` (TODO 1.291, partial): the Appwrite source limits now trace the utopia-php/database Mongo, Memory, Redis and Pool adapters at the commit already cited, as source reading. Mongo, Memory and Redis `getDocument` add no authorization check, and their `find` permission filters (Mongo's `$in` on `_permissions`, Memory's role-index allow set and Redis's per-action role sets) apply only while authorization is enabled. Pool delegates both methods after setting its own authorization instance on the pooled adapter, and Memory and Redis extend `Utopia\Database\Adapter`. For Appwrite 2.2.0, `_APP_DB_ADAPTER` (since 1.9.0) defaults to `postgresql`, and the pool registration maps `mariadb`, `mongodb`, `mysql` and `postgresql` to the MariaDB, Mongo, MySQL and Postgres adapters, with DocumentsDB limited to `mongodb`. On the document GET's non-transaction retrieval path, the database factory wraps pools in `Pool`. The existing read-path text and `appwrite-read-path` claim are now scoped to that path, and transaction-state retrieval is not traced. The development `.env` value is labelled a development default. The root Compose file is described as the template the install task reads, and it passes `_APP_DB_ADAPTER` without a value. The upgrade task assumes MariaDB when the variable is absent, and the current environment documentation's conflicting `mongodb` default is recorded. Six new claims carry this (claims 80 to 85, with `source-limits` rewritten from 354 to 355 characters). Collection-grant and disabled-status consequences stay this guide's reasoning. Three gaps stay open under TODO 1.291: which adapter a given deployment uses, the installer's selection flow, and the document GET's transaction-state retrieval. The console's `mariadb` fallback gets new row 1.332, and live authorization is not demonstrated. (#798)
- `elasticsearch.md` (TODO 1.297, partial): the OpenSearch GCS/Azure client-side-encryption checked range now runs from 2.10.0 to 3.9.0. All of the following is source reading without an OpenSearch run. `BlobStoreProvider` is cited at 3.4.0 and 3.9.0. Its `createBlobStore` wraps the repository's store in `EncryptedBlobStore` when server-side encryption is off and repository crypto metadata is non-null, and the 3.5.0 to 3.7.0 files share 3.4.0's git blob ID. `CryptoSettings.java` matches the cited 2.10.0, 3.0.0 or 3.8.0 file by git blob ID at every checked tag, so `validate()` requires both key-provider fields at all 31 tags. Git tree listings at the 41 release tags from 1.0.0 to 2.9.0 contain no `EncryptedBlobStore`, `CryptoSettings` or `crypto-kms` path. The 2.9.0 `blobStore()`, `PutRepositoryRequest` and `RepositoryMetadata` are cited without crypto handling under the new `repo290` component. No release tag after 3.9.0 existed when the tag list was checked on 2026-10-22. `BlobStoreRepository` has no native-store reference at 3.7.0 or 3.9.0. At 3.9.0, `IndexService` is the only non-test `getNativeStore()` caller, for a warm index's remote store repository when the writable-warm feature flag, a warm index, a pluggable data format and a format-aware store directory factory are all present. The index-level `CryptoMetadata` is traced through `EncryptedBlobContainer` (3.5.0 to 3.9.0), which encrypts with the repository-level handler and forwards it. It is also traced through `AzureBlobContainer` (3.5.0 to 3.9.0), which the 3.9.0 `AzureBlobStore` returns as its blob container and which throws `UnsupportedOperationException` for a non-null value. At 3.9.0 only, it is traced through the GCS container and `BlobContainer`'s default, which drop it, and `CryptoMetadata.fromIndexSettings` returns null unless `index.store.crypto.key_provider` is set. Seven claims are added, bringing the claim count from 74 to 81. `os-gcs-azure-versions`, `-blobstore`, `-range` and `-index` are revised, the pre-existing `os-gcs-azure-core` is joined into two sentences, and every other qualifier is kept. Still open under 1.297: deployed encryption, installed providers, later releases, repository code before 2.10.0 beyond the absent class paths (code was read only at 2.9.0), native-store encryption and its callers at 3.7.0 and 3.8.0, GCS handling at 3.5.0 to 3.8.0, effective behaviour and a universal deployed minimum. ## 2026-10-20

- Carried QA notes from earlier PRs: `agent-builders.md` rewords the body sentence on the console permission read to a type-only statement and adds a `debug-permission-read` claim citing Dify's `usePluginPermissionSettings`, which GETs `/workspaces/current/plugin/permission/fetch` typed as `Permissions`, a compile-time type that declares `install_permission` and `debug_permission` but does not prove the runtime fields (claims 63 to 64); `n8n.md` cites the controller-activation code and the controller registry's authentication middleware, which returns 401 `Unauthorized` on every controller route when the auth registry does not authenticate, and adds a `public-controller-order` claim that, as this guide's inference, a controller can respond before the validator's security check without skipping authentication (claims 37 to 38); `paas.md` rewrites three neither/nor constructions with three or more items; the `machine-auth.md` note (cite `crypto.py#L1`) is closed without change because neither the `key-hash` claim nor its body sentence states that import; the `identity-providers.md` Okta wording note stays open because its source was not available to check a rewording (#734)
- `vector-databases.md`: hosted-console account security now cites Weaviate Cloud's single-sign-on documentation source (the multi-factor page at the same commit was already cited) and Qdrant Cloud's account-setup documentation source at a newer commit: Weaviate Cloud's documentation source says console SSO is SAML, not OIDC, and that it stops email/password and Google sign-in after a person's first SSO sign-in, turns MFA enforcement off and never prompts for a Weaviate Cloud MFA code, while Qdrant Cloud's lists OpenID Connect and SAML console SSO for the Premium tier, enabled through a support ticket, with no console MFA control in the cited sources; as this guide's inference, MFA with Weaviate Cloud SSO depends on the identity provider, and the advice to require an MFA-enforcing identity provider wherever SSO is used and to confirm enforcement is kept, and the OIDC advice is scoped to self-hosted Weaviate's database settings, not Weaviate Cloud console SSO; the sources are documentation source files whose publication and live-console behaviour are not established, and the Weaviate MFA page's UI labels are not treated as verified; five new claims carry this (claims 59 to 64), and Pinecone MFA enforcement, deployed identity-provider enforcement, any Qdrant Cloud console MFA control, publication and release timing, the Weaviate MFA enforcement UI details and Zilliz stay open under TODO 1.312 (#733)
- `agent-builders.md`: the Dify plugin-debugging remedy now cites, at the commit already pinned, the legacy fallback workspace permission lists and role mapping, which give `plugin.plugin_preferences` to owner and admin but not editor, normal or dataset_operator when external RBAC is disabled, and `CategoryMarketplacePanel`, which imports the plugin-settings access hook and wires only the install button from it, when the panel is expanded and neither loading nor showing an installed-ID error; the client-side flags `canSetPermissions`, `canSetPluginPreferences` and `canDebugPlugin` are described as UI flags, not server enforcement; as this guide's assessment, the cited files do not establish role assignments under external or enterprise RBAC or a permission or preferences editor, which stay open under TODO 1.283; `debug-remedy` is rewritten (396 to 337 characters) and five new claims carry the rest (claims 58 to 63), and the advice to use the permission-change API when debugging is needed, or disable the listener otherwise, is kept (#732)
- `n8n.md`: Code-node environment exposure now cites the n8n 2.40.6 task runners and task-runner-launcher 1.5.1, pinned to commits: as this guide's reasoning, the JS runner's context binds no `process` directly; its data proxy falls back to an empty `env` when no environment state arrives; secure mode runs all-items and per-item code with `runInContext` and insecure mode with `new Function`, which, as this guide's reasoning, can resolve the ambient `process.env` unless shadowed (not a live demonstration, and not proof of complete isolation in secure mode); the Python runner clears `os.environ` before user code only when `runner_env_deny` is true; the launcher inherits only its own `LANG`, `PATH`, `TZ` and `TERM` plus names in `allowed-env`, then adds runtime values (broker URI and health-check values, plus legacy timeout values) and permitted `env-overrides`, and does not inherit required runtime keys such as the grant token; this guide advises keeping secrets out of `allowed-env`; eight new claims carry this (claims 29 to 37), the `$env` guard advice is kept, and incoming environment-state production and contents, the Python setting's default and effective module access, complete `process.env` isolation, internal-mode runners, other nodes, other launcher versions and deployed configuration stay open under TODO 1.300 (#731)
- `pocketbase.md`: the Appwrite source limits now trace the utopia-php/database SQL adapter at the commit already cited: `getDocument` in `SQL.php` (used by MariaDB, MySQL, Postgres and SQLite) adds no authorization check of its own, `find` takes the shared authorization roles and adds a document-permission predicate only while authorization is enabled, and Postgres overrides the predicate with JSONB containment tests; as this guide's reasoning, a collection grant skips that filter, and disabled authorization drops the find permission filter while query and shared-table tenant conditions remain; four new claims carry this (claims 75 to 79, with `source-limits` rewritten from 407 to 354 characters); the Mongo, Redis, Memory and Pool adapters and which adapter a deployment uses stay open under TODO 1.291, and live authorization is not demonstrated (#730)
- `machine-auth.md`: the image-layer scan in the artifact check now cites Trivy v0.76.0 documentation pinned to a commit: `trivy image` with `--scanners secret` scans files inside the image, and Trivy tries to detect a base image and skip those layers for secret scanning, so, as this guide's assessment, a secret in a base layer may not be reported; allowed paths, allow rules, `--skip-dirs` and `--skip-files` can exclude content and `trivy-secret.yaml` can change the rules; as this guide's assessment, a clean result does not prove absence of keys; detection coverage for this project's key format, behaviour in other releases and image-config secret scanning stay open under TODO 1.299; three new claims carry this (claims 37 to 40), `verify-artifacts` no longer says Sources has no image-layer scanning tool, and no command or expectation changed (#729)
- `self-hosted-idp.md`: the authentik administration boundary now cites authentik version/2026.8.3 source instead of saying Sources has no reference: as this guide's assessment, the listen defaults (HTTP `[::]:9000`, HTTPS `[::]:9443`, metrics `[::]:9300`) and Go startup show no separate administration listener, which is not an exhaustive proof of deployed topology; the interface views show no administrator-permission check for loading the admin template; API v3 route registration, `TokenAuthentication` and the RBAC permission classes are cited with their limits; and the core URLs give admin-related paths to investigate (`/if/admin/`, `/if/user/`, flows and `/ws/` routes) rather than a complete restriction list; the earlier, broader assessment of shared listeners and reliance on the permission model is narrowed to these statements, with the permission boundary left unestablished; six new claims carry these (claims 55 to 61), the proxy path-restriction advice is kept, the unused `authentik-docs:scc8077354dac` key is removed from the component map while its Sources entry stays, and the effective API prefix, session authentication, endpoint permission wiring, HTTPS serving, deployed listener topology, a complete path list and the metrics-listener follow-up stay open under TODO 1.304 (#728)
- `observability-components.md`: Loki v3.7.8 deletion admission is now traced to the mode: `validDeletionLimit` parses the tenant's deletion mode, only `disabled`, `filter-only` and `filter-and-delete` parse, `DeleteEnabled` is true for the latter two, and `TenantMiddleware` returns 400 for tenant or parsing errors and 403 for `disabled` before the create, list and cancel handlers, as source reasoning rather than observed responses (new `loki-delete-mode` claim; `loki-delete` no longer calls the mapping unverified); the querier worker service's address fallback and the worker and processor dial sites are cited in new `loki-worker-init` and `loki-worker-dials` claims, `loki-internal` is shortened from 500 to 397 characters, loopback stays this guide's configuration-specific advice, and the remaining internal-path and runtime evidence stays open under TODO 1.301 (claims 59 to 62) (#727)
- `elasticsearch.md`: OpenSearch GCS/Azure client-side encryption now cites OpenSearch 2.10.0 and 3.0.0 pinned to commits (repository `CryptoSettings` validation, the `EncryptedBlobStore` wrap in `BlobStoreRepository` when crypto metadata is present, and GCS/Azure store creation), the 2.10.0 release notes listing the crypto library, encryption SDK and `crypto-kms` plugin, and 2.9.0 source directories without those entries; as this guide's assessment, 2.10.0 is the earliest checked release with repository crypto settings and the wrap, while releases between those checked, a universal minimum for deployed GCS/Azure encryption, whether both repositories reach the wrap at 2.10.0 and 3.0.0, installed key providers and live behaviour stay open under TODO 1.297; the `os-gcs-azure` claim (967 characters) is split into six claims of at most 365 characters, each citing the sources for its own text (claims 65 to 70) (#726)
- `n8n.md`: the public-API status expectations now rest on a traced, labelled assessment for n8n 2.40.6: the auth registry returns the first non-null strategy result or `false`, the `ApiKeyAuth`, `BearerAuth` and `CookieAuth` handlers return it, and n8n's pinned express-openapi-validator 5.5.3 (a new `validator` component) checks the scheme before the handler, throwing an error without a status when a header `apiKey` is missing; the guide assesses that a selected security failure without a status (a `false` result or that error) gets fallback status 401, which n8n's error path serializes, while other exceptions can supply other statuses; public controllers are mounted before the validator, so a controller-owned route can respond before its security check; the cited workflows handler only returns a workflow version, and `GET /api/v1/workflows` route security and list success stay uncited and open under TODO 1.308, and rejection (for example 401) and 200 workflow JSON stay expectations to observe, with the fronting-proxy caution kept (#725)

- `ai-infra-services.md`: Onyx `AUTH_TYPE` now also cites v4.5.0 and v4.9.2 `main.py`, where startup still calls `verify_auth_setting()` (commented as a warning on stale `AUTH_TYPE` values) and, inside `if not MULTI_TENANT`, `seed_saml_provider_from_conf_dir(db_session)` (commented as a no-op unless `AUTH_TYPE=saml` with no SAML row yet); that these legacy startup calls were not removed by v4.9.2 is this guide's assessment, in a new `onyx-auth-type-legacy-startup` claim (claims 47 to 48), while `onyx-auth-type` keeps the v4.3.13/v4.4.0 inference; the tags are not commit pins, and runtime authentication selection at those tags and the September 2026 record of a planned v4.5 removal stay open under TODO 1.311; the citation-pinning note now limits its commit-pin and confirmation statement to the LangServe README, Mem0 server, Onyx Compose and Onyx data-model citations and says the Onyx version-tag citations use tags (#723)
- `ollama.md`: the first-party source review the guide already reported is re-run over every non-test v0.34.2 Go file (still no `net/http/pprof` import, pprof handler registration or `/debug/pprof` route), and its result is now stated as this guide's assessment that first-party code registers no pprof handler; whether a module dependency registers one, whether the served mux exposes it, and shipped-binary pprof availability stay open under TODO 1.302, and the guide still says to observe the actual binary before relying on the source trace (#722)
- `identity-providers.md`: per-provider TOTP compatibility now cites, in three new per-provider claims, Microsoft Entra's pinned OATH-token page (third-party OATH TOTP apps with an Entra-generated seed; programmable hardware tokens; the hardware preview's SHA-1/SHA-256 and 30- or 60-second periods; no HOTP), Okta's help pages (Google Authenticator's six-digit TOTP once added by an admin; Custom TOTP with per-user seeds and matching HMAC and encoding settings) and Google's Google Authenticator help (codes for 2-Step Verification sign-in); the Google prompt, Microsoft Authenticator and Okta Verify pages stay cited, and other providers and arbitrary apps remain this guide's assessment under TODO 1.307 (#721)
- `machine-auth.md`: the advice to store a hash of a random API key now cites one example implementation, djangorestframework-api-key v3.1.0 with Django 5.2.18: SHA-512 over the key, hex-encoded with a `sha512$$` prefix and no salt (which the library justifies only for high-entropy keys), a constant-time comparison, and a 32-character secret drawn with `secrets.choice` from 62 characters, about 190.5 bits by this guide's calculation; it is presented as an example, not a standard, and new advice limits fast hashes to high-entropy random keys, not passwords (TODO 1.298) (#720)
- `vector-databases.md`: hosted-console MFA now cites Weaviate Cloud's pinned documentation (per-account MFA and Owner-enforced MFA for all members, both off by default; after a member's first SSO sign-in, SSO takes priority over MFA enforcement) and Qdrant Cloud's pinned Premium-tier console SSO, distinct from cluster API keys, and retains Pinecone's organization SSO citation (Standard and Enterprise plans, with console re-authentication at least every 24 hours), now time-qualified with its own claim; SSO is not presented as MFA, and the guide keeps its advice to use an MFA-enforcing identity provider wherever SSO is the sign-in path. No Zilliz source is cited. Pinecone MFA enforcement, identity-provider MFA behind those SSO options, and any Qdrant Cloud or Zilliz console MFA control stay open under TODO 1.312 (#719)
- `oidc-integration.md`: the GitHub organization-membership check now also cites the pinned GitHub Enterprise Server page on required 2FA (members without 2FA keep their membership but cannot access enterprise resources until they enable it; outside collaborators without 2FA are removed), alongside the organization page already cited; the claim keeps its premise, inference, assessment and advice labels. Whether the organization-membership API, or an app's membership check, still treats such a member as a member is not shown, and that gap stays open under TODO 1.310; because those policies retain members without 2FA, the guide's inference that membership alone does not prove MFA stands (#718)
- `python.md`: the CA-bundle claim is split into three (`client-ca` for CPython's `ssl`, `client-ca-httpx`, `client-ca-advice` for requests and the export advice), each mapping only its own sources. It now cites CPython v3.14.8's `_ssl.c` (`set_default_verify_paths` calls OpenSSL's `SSL_CTX_set_default_verify_paths`; `get_default_verify_paths` reports the default paths and environment-variable names), and, alongside the OpenSSL 4.0.3 documentation already cited for `SSL_CERT_FILE`, now also cites it for `SSL_CERT_DIR` and for OpenSSL's qualifier that these are not consulted for set-user-ID or set-group-ID programs; that CPython's call uses them is this guide's inference. The OpenSSL a given CPython build links, platform-specific default stores and deployed behaviour stay open under TODO 1.303 (#717)
- `self-signed.md`: the trust-store commands now also cite Debian bookworm and Ubuntu jammy (`update-ca-certificates`), and CentOS Stream 8 and 10 and the Fedora rawhide development branch (`update-ca-trust`), each labelled for what it is (CentOS Stream is not RHEL; rawhide is not a released Fedora); the Debian trixie, Ubuntu noble and RHEL 9 citations stay, and other releases, including RHEL 8 and 10 and released Fedora, remain this guide's assessment under TODO 1.306 (#716)
- `self-hosted-idp.md`: the Docker socket scope for authentik's shipped Compose worker now cites the pinned Docker documentation that the daemon requires root unless it runs in rootless mode (where the daemon and containers run without root privileges) and that only trusted users should control it; that ordinary rootful socket access is host-root-equivalent, and that a rootless daemon's blast radius is its user's, remain this guide's assessments (TODO 1.305) (#715)
- `authentication.md`: the transport-authentication rule now cites OWASP's GraphQL, REST and WebSocket cheat sheets (at the pin the guide already uses) for per-transport access control, and the Standard Webhooks specification's signed message identifiers and ID deduplication, as guidance rather than established deployment behaviour; rejecting repeats within the timestamp window stays this guide's requirement. Still open under TODO 1.294: the HTML-login boundary for non-HTTP transports, general GraphQL authentication beyond introspection advice, authentication boundaries for non-REST inference and management APIs, REST-style inference and management controls (guide requirements based on generic REST guidance), and comprehensive webhook replay rejection (#714)
- `docker.md`: the `workload` claim is shortened from 491 to 421 characters in two sentences, labelling its advice explicitly and grouping its two assessments under one label, with its components and sources unchanged (TODO 1.317) (#713)

## 2026-10-19

- `paas.md`: the HTTPS-redirect evidence now quotes Render's TLS and custom-domains pages, which say HTTP is automatically redirected to HTTPS (no status code stated), separately from Render's static-site redirect rules, whose 301 is a different feature, and Heroku's ACM handling of Let's Encrypt challenge URLs; Railway's GET 301 and POST conversion stay cited, and the claim keeps the advice to redirect in the app unless deployed ingress is known to enforce HTTPS. Render's redirect status, Railway's HEAD redirect behaviour and POST status, Heroku's redirect policy, configuration/application responsibility and status, and header sanitization stay open under TODO 1.268 (#712)
- `agent-builders.md`: the Dify debug-permission remedy now cites more of the pinned console code: the console UI returns `canSetPermissions: !rbacEnabled && canSetPluginPreferences`, where `canSetPluginPreferences` is `hasPermission(workspacePermissionKeys, 'plugin.plugin_preferences')`, and its debug flag from `plugin.debug` when Enterprise RBAC is on or from `debug_permission` otherwise; these are client-side flags, while the server change endpoint requires `is_admin_or_owner` (which returns true when `RBAC_ENABLED` is true and otherwise checks for a privileged role). Which roles hold the key and where the control is rendered are not shown by the cited files and stay open under TODO 1.283; the guide's advice remains the API call (#711)
- `docker.md`: keeping `.env` out of Git now has its own claim: keep it in `.gitignore` and untrack it if already tracked (citing Git v2.49.0 `git rm --cached`, which removes paths only from the index and leaves working tree files alone), and rotate any secret that was ever committed, since untracking does not remove it from earlier commits, labelled as this guide's advice and reasoning. The `workload` claim keeps its other text and drops its Git ignore mapping; shortening it is TODO 1.317 (#710)
- `pocketbase.md`: Appwrite's document-read authorization is now traced into the pinned utopia-php/database 7.3.9 code: how read permissions are extracted and matched against requester roles, `getDocument`'s collection read check (with document read roles added when `documentSecurity` is set), `find`'s collection check and dispatch to the adapter, and the authorization status and the instance's `skip`. What the adapter's `getDocument` and `find` do with the shared authorization instance, including any document-permission filtering, and what a disabled status bypasses there stay open under TODO 1.291 (#709)
- `nodejs.md`, `python.md` and `paas.md`: the Heroku Fir statements (IPv6 as the default for networking within Private Spaces, the warning that apps designed assuming IPv4-only connectivity do not bind properly on Fir, and the IPv6 or dual-stack listener recommendation) now quote Heroku's Generations article, and `paas.md`'s statement that adding custom domains requires a verified Heroku account quotes the custom-domains article; TODO 1.315 and 1.316 are closed (#708)
- `gpu-clouds.md`: the account-MFA advice now cites what each platform's pinned or current documentation shows: RunPod's quickstart recommends two-factor authentication, and its account-management page documents team roles; Vast.ai documents authenticator-app (TOTP) or SMS two-factor methods with email as a backup; Lambda's console lets users set up MFA; Modal's Workspaces page notes accounts can be provisioned through SAML SSO and documents workspace roles, and Modal's security page requires MFA for Modal's own employees only, so where a Modal workspace offers SSO the guide advises an identity provider that enforces MFA. - `nodejs.md`, `python.md` and `paas.md`: Railway's listener contract is now cited from its pinned documentation (bind `0.0.0.0` on the injected `PORT`; its edge proxy terminates TLS, and traffic is then forwarded over Railway's internal network to the deployment), and `paas.md`'s redirect bullet links the Railway redirect source inline. Heroku Cedar's listener address, Fly's automatic certificate renewal, the Vercel Functions listener contract and the ACM Private Spaces wildcard question stay open; new TODO rows 1.315 and 1.316 cover two Heroku statements whose cited articles are not yet checked (#704)
- `docker.md`: the secrets rule now cites pinned Docker documentation for environment-variable risks, `env_file`, Compose secrets and Swarm secrets, and reuses Git's v2.49.0 gitignore citation. It describes Compose secrets as files under `/run/secrets/`, supported on Linux containers only, with access granted per service. Swarm secrets are restricted to swarm services; the secrets are encrypted in transit and at rest, accessible to explicitly authorized services while their tasks run, and mounted in memory in Linux containers. The guide's reasoning distinguishes defining a value in `.env` from setting the container environment, with `environment` and `env_file` examples and interpolation elsewhere in Compose. Offering environment files and ignoring or untracking `.env` are labelled as guide advice; the weaker standing of environment files is labelled as guide assessment. The claim retains the history-source limit; TODO 1.296 moves to DONE, and new row 1.314 carries the `workload` claim's `.gitignore` labelling, untracking and rotation follow-up (#703)

## 2026-10-18

- `nodejs.md` cites its password and MFA advice: OWASP's Password Storage cheat sheet at its pinned commit for preferring Argon2id and limiting bcrypt to legacy systems where Argon2 and scrypt are unavailable, with a work factor of at least 10 and a 72-byte input limit; `node-argon2` v0.45.1 for Argon2id support and default; and `node.bcrypt.js` v6.0.0 for the CommonJS import, `hash` and `compare`, Promise returns without a callback, salt generation from a numeric cost and `2^cost` iterations, with cost 12 labelled this guide's example choice; for MFA it keeps the otplib and qrcode TOTP option and names Cloudflare Access independent MFA as the fronting option, citing the effective-policy precedence and origin protection at the existing pinned commit, and requires checking the effective policy, testing that access without the second factor is denied and keeping the origin unreachable around Access, stating that the pages do not establish a deployment's policy or origin isolation (rows 1.274 and 1.275) (#672)
- `dotnet.md` names a concrete fronting MFA option: it keeps enforcing MFA at the identity provider as a requirement and recommends Cloudflare Access with independent MFA for a self-hosted public application only where the effective policy requires MFA and the origin cannot be reached around Access, citing Cloudflare's docs at the existing pinned commit for the authentication layer, the independent-MFA factors, organization-level enablement and the policy over application over organization precedence, and the need to protect a publicly routable origin; the guide states that these pages do not establish a deployment's effective policy or origin isolation (row 1.284) (#671)

- `tools/check_bare_urls.py` uses the pinned CommonMark parser to reject uncancelled HTTP(S) URLs containing `__` in tracked root Markdown bodies. It counts raw inline-block occurrences; a parse-confirmed autolink removes only its own occurrence, while an inline link or image destination cancels a whole raw occurrence only when a sentinel swap preserves the full inline child stream except for that destination. Parser-encoded URL-ending characters cannot cancel a separately written encoded bare URL; author-written encodings remain exempt. Reference-definition destinations are not subtracted. CONTRIBUTING records boundary-character and escaped-parenthesis extra findings, with fixtures; local runs without the pin use an advisory lexical fallback, and CI requires the pin. The `kafka.md` KIP-801 citation becomes an autolink with its URL unchanged (row 3.42); row 3.41 tracks the pinned-source URL gate (#623)

- `realtime-voice-infra.md` cites LiveKit v1.13.7 and the `livekit/protocol` commit it requires (a4f4b5c0) for webhook verification: static `webhook.urls` receive an `Authorization` JWT signed with the configured `webhook.api_key` secret, while an extra webhook may select a different `SigningKey` and secret; the `sha256` claim holds the standard-base64 SHA-256 hash of the body, and protocol `webhook.Receive` verifies the token and compares that claim with the body's hash (row 1.179) (#570)
- `apache.md` cites Debian apache2 2.4.69-1 packaging (`a2enmod`, the `a2ensite` link, `default-ssl`, `htpasswd` in `apache2-utils`), Fedora f44 httpd packaging at commit 6df70d39 (`mod_ssl`, and `htpasswd` in `httpd-tools`), httpd 2.4.69 for `htpasswd -B` (default cost 5, `-C` sets it), the OWASP Password Storage Cheat Sheet at commit 72865b1e for the bcrypt work factor of at least 10, Authelia v4.39.28 for Apache being unsupported, and mod_auth_openidc v2.4.20.3 for its OIDC relying-party role; provider MFA stays the guide's advice and Cloudflare Access stays stated as unsourced (row 1.179) (#572)
- `self-hosted-error-trackers.md` cites GlitchTip backend v6.2.6 for its membership statement (on login, a user with a linked social account for a provider that has an organization social app is added to that organization), and `realtime-webhooks.md` cites Caddy v2.11.4 for the `request_body` `max_size` body limit (413 on excess) and for the standard HTTP module list importing no rate-limit handler (row 1.179) (#569)
- `workflow-orchestrators.md` sources four of its row 1.179 facts in pinned upstream code: Prefect 3.1.8 block-document reads accept `include_secrets`, the Airflow FAB provider 3.9.0 reads `[fab] auth_role_public` (or the legacy `AUTH_ROLE_PUBLIC`) for its public-role checks, Airflow 3.3.2 uses no encryption when the Fernet key is empty, and the Temporal Go SDK v1.28.0 codec HTTP handler does no authentication itself; the key-generation statement the fetched files do not establish is removed (#567)

- `supabase-self-hosted.md` sources its row 1.179 `.env.example` defaults line by line at commit eabe06be (database, JWT, dashboard, S3, encryption, pooler, signup and Functions values; the demo `ANON_KEY` and `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` verified locally as HS256-signed with the demo `JWT_SECRET`), cites the Compose and Kong lines that wire them, and narrows the dashboard-route statement the fetched files do not establish (#565)

- chat-uis.md: the anonymous API Verify probe now prints its status with `curl -q -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n'` instead of `-s` printing only the body, so a pass needs a visible 401 and a transport error shows curl's error rather than empty output; the proxy probe moves from `-sI` to `-sSI` so a TLS, DNS or connection failure is no longer silent. The curl Sources item now cites the manual for `-s`, `-S`, `-o /dev/null`, `-I` and `http_code`, and `verify-login` cites it. A local run with curl 8.18.0 against loopback stubs answering 401 and 200 and a closed port showed the API probe's before and after output, including `000`, which is recorded as observed because the manual does not document it. A closed-port run of the proxy probe showed `-sI` silent and `-sSI` printing curl's connection error. Follow-up from #465's review (#467).

## 2026-10-12

- cloud-identity-proxies.md, cors.md: Verify probes now show the status line, so empty output cannot read as a pass (follow-up from #463's review). In cloud-identity-proxies.md the forged-header `/whoami` probe adds `-D -`, the anonymous HEAD probe uses `-sSI`, and the altered-assertion example now builds its `x-amzn-oidc-data` header with `printf` and feeds it to curl on stdin with `--header @-` from a rule-7 subshell, never on the command line. In cors.md each probe runs in its own `set -o pipefail` subshell with an `|| echo` inconclusive line, so a failed or partial transfer cannot read as a pass, and the hostile-origin expectation requires a 2xx status matching the own-origin probe, treating any other status, a proxy's lone Connection established line, or empty output as inconclusive. Each guide's Verify declaration records the curl 8.18.0 runs against a loopback listener and a closed loopback port that showed the before and after output (#465).
- mfa.md, self-hosted-error-trackers.md: three version-basis notes now name exactly what remains uncited (store TODO 47, batch 5). mfa `push-matching` also names the prompt-bombing rationale for number matching as uncited; `webauthn-recovery` no longer says Sources has no recovery-policy reference (the claim cites the NIST look-up-secrets and OWASP recovery-code entries) and names only the second-hardware-key preference for administrators as uncited (the NIST look-up-secrets entry covers code phishability and rate limiting, and the same document's section 4.2.3 requires recovery notification); self-hosted-error-trackers `membership` now says only the GlitchTip membership implementation is uncited (the Sentry registration and single-organization entry is cited). Claim status and citations are unchanged (#466).
- authentication.md, observability-components.md: resolve the version-basis notes left by #461's review (store TODO 47, batch 4). authentication `session-cookies` now names the OWASP session-cookie attributes source; `offboarding` also names the measure-and-shrink time-to-revoke practice as uncited; `secret-storage` drops the scanner half of its note, which the pinned Gitleaks entry covers; `federated-allowlist` no longer says Sources has no Microsoft reference (the Entra entry covers session revocation, not tenant validation); `verify-secret-scan` is unchanged. observability-components `loki-grpc-auth` and its body caveat no longer say the auth-helper implementation is missing, since Sources list Loki's auth setup and fake_auth.go; the claim and the body keep the untraced interceptor caveat. Claim status and citations are unchanged (#464).
- mfa.md, paas.md, self-hosted-error-trackers.md, dotnet.md, go.md, python.md, gpu-clouds.md, ray.md, nginx.md, free-certificates.md: twenty-six version-basis claims drop or narrow a stale note that a source is missing from Sources (store TODO 47, batch 3: mfa `mfa-enforcement`, `push-matching`, `totp-activation`, `recovery-codes`, `factor-changes`, `authelia-factors`, `totp-libraries`, `ssh-pam-enforcement`, `duo-unix-failmode`, `supabase-assurance`, `access-mfa`, `verify-mfa-operation` and `verify-offboarding`; paas `public-env`, `proxy-awareness` and `database-identity`; self-hosted-error-trackers `sentry-secret` and `sentry-nginx`; dotnet, go and python `mfa`; gpu-clouds `verify-inventory`; ray `serve-grpc`; nginx `hsts` and `hsts-inheritance`; free-certificates `alpn-challenge`). Each claim already cites a component source that Sources lists. Eight notes are narrowed rather than deleted, to what remains uncited: nginx `hsts-inheritance` (add_header inheritance and `always` coverage), free-certificates `alpn-challenge` (no Caddy TLS-ALPN-01 source), mfa `push-matching` (verified push, the non-phishing-resistance of TOTP and push, and the email or SMS ranking), `authelia-factors` (passkeys) and `verify-offboarding` (enrolled second factors and app-level authorizations), dotnet and go `mfa` (the fronting identity layer) and gpu-clouds `verify-inventory` (network-namespace scope). - cloud-identity-proxies.md: two Verify probes that print only a response body now use `curl -q -sS` instead of `-s` (the forged-header `/whoami` check and the inline `x-amzn-oidc-data` example). With `-s` alone a transport error printed nothing, which reads like the expected rejection; `-S` prints curl's error text. - tools/check_changelog_prs.py: the changelog gate now reads merge-commit pull requests (`Merge pull request #N from ...`) as well as squash `(#N)` suffixes (maintainer ruling 2026-10-12). This repository has merged with merge commits since #299, and through #459 the old gate read none of those 160 merge-commit subjects. It still checked #303 and #306, but only because a commit on each one's branch ended in its `(#N)`, so 158 merged pull requests were never checked while the gate still passed: the old gate counted 299, and on the full history through #459 the gate now counts 457, all referenced. Ten new cases, seven of which fail against the old gate, cover both shapes, an anchored prefix that requires GitHub's `from` after the number, the exemption following the highest number rather than the latest merge, and merges of main into a branch, which do not count (#460).
- apache.md, nginx.md, haproxy.md, free-certificates.md, devops-uis.md, docker.md, web-exposure.md: eleven version-basis claims no longer say a source is missing from Sources (store TODO 47, batch 1: apache `certbot` and `hsts`, nginx `certbot`, haproxy `basic`, free-certificates `server-automation`, `verify-https` and `verify-certificate`, devops-uis `registry-bcrypt`, docker `private-network` and `tls`, web-exposure `caddy-acme`). Each claim already cites a component source that Sources lists; claim status and citations are unchanged. Two free-certificates.md Verify declarations that said the curl and OpenSSL sources were not recorded now name the cited documentation (#459).
- transactional-email-posture.md and web-exposure.md: three version-basis claims no longer say a source is missing (`tls-validation`, `caddy-listing`, `verify-acme`), because each already cites the component source now listed in Sources (RFC 8997 section 3; Caddy v2.11.4 file_server browse; RFC 8555 section 8.3). Claim status and citations are unchanged (#458).
- cloud-identity-proxies.md: the Verify check of the app's own default platform route is now a guarded block under its list item, with the whole-block marker, a quoted value count and a placeholder refusal, so an unsubstituted paste sends no request (row 1.194, found in #454's review). The item and the block declare REASONED with the local guard cases that were run, and the item's marking exemption is removed (#457).
- realtime-voice-infra.md: derive a coturn static-user key without the TURN password in any process argument (row 1.193). At coturn 4.18.0 `turnadmin -k` takes the password only in argv, so a guarded Bash block instead reads it silently, pipes `user:realm:password` from the builtin `printf` to `openssl dgst -md5 -r`, checks the pipeline and the 32-digit hex result, and prints the `user=NAME:0xKEY` line. Locally it reproduced coturn's example key `bc807ee29df3c9ffa736523fb2c4e8ee`, and an instrumented run recorded no password in any command line; it accepts only ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`. Sources gain the OpenSSL 3.5 dgst manual (#455).
- search-engines.md: the credential scanner's limits now say that a key present only in a commit or tag message, an author, committer or tagger identity, a reflog message, a ref name or a path name is not searched, and `tools/test_scan_credential.py` gains six documented-limit cases showing each returns NOT FOUND (314 named cases in all). The scanner itself is unchanged; this came from the second-family review of #450 (#456).
- Add scoped REASONED Verify declarations across thirteen guides (row 3.30, batch 4): code-server, fronting auth, self-signed certificates, transactional email posture, web exposure, BI dashboards, cloud firewalls, cloud identity proxies, headers, identity providers, Kubernetes, self-hosted Supabase and Tailscale. Remove 29 marking exemptions (6 list items and 23 prose units) by subtraction-only regeneration, from 735 to 706 retained units; eleven of the guides leave the baseline entirely. Two units stay: a cloud-firewalls.md prose unit, because its Sources have no entry for the IAP broker range and the Azure Bastion exception it states, and a cloud-identity-proxies.md list item whose inline command passes an unguarded placeholder, which new row 1.194 tracks for a separate fix. Declarations cite the guides' existing Sources and record the local checks that were run (shell syntax and lint, input-guard cases with network commands replaced by local stubs, YAML and JSON parsing, offline certificate generation and verification, an endpoint parser and synthetic token encoding) together with what those checks do not establish; preserve commands, claims and recorded limitations (#454).
- search-engines.md: the Verify section's credential check is now a reviewed repository tool, `tools/scan_credential.sh` (maintainer ruling, 2026-10-12: tool first, no paste-in block), and the guide points to it. Using only Git and Bash builtins, it reads the admin, master or bootstrap key silently, passes it to `git grep` through a pipe so it is never a command-line argument, and searches the regular files of the client build output and the regular-file content of every commit from `git rev-list --all --reflog` for the literal key, printing matching names only. It reports FOUND, NOT FOUND or INCONCLUSIVE with distinct exit statuses. It reports INCONCLUSIVE when it could not look in the ways it checks for: an empty output directory, or a symlinked, unreadable or special entry or a `.git` component in it; any Git diagnostic or abnormal status; a `GIT_` variable it cannot remove; or `printf`, `read`, `compgen` or `git` not resolving as intended. It removes every `GIT_` variable before running Git and runs Git without a pager. It is for a fresh shell with trusted startup files and Git configuration, and does not defend against a shell that redefines Bash's keywords. Its regression suite, `tools/test_scan_credential.py`, runs 308 named cases on disposable repositories as part of the gate suite, with ShellCheck on the script; a skip is allowed only for a named case and reason and otherwise fails the suite, and eleven cases sample the argv and environment of the scanner and its children during live scans and find no key, which is bounded sampling, not proof. It was not run against a real application build. Its stated limits are substantial and a NOT FOUND covers only the stated scope: no gzip or Brotli decoding; no encoded, escaped or split keys; committed symbolic-link targets, submodules, LFS, objects present in no commit tree, original content or ancestry hidden by replacement refs or grafts, unreachable objects, unfetched or shallow history and refs local to another worktree are not searched; and a file changed during the scan can be skipped. The Git manual is added to Sources, and one stale Verify-marking baseline entry for the replaced prose is removed. Row 1.117 stays open for the real-build controls (#450).
- TODO.md and DECISIONS.md: add backlog row 1.193, a coturn key derivation that keeps the TURN password off the command line (maintainer ruling 2026-10-12), and record in DECISIONS.md that pull request bodies carry no AI attribution line, superseding the 2026-09-19 entry's clause about pull request bodies (#453).
- vector-databases.md: say how the Milvus Helm chart and the Milvus Operator expose the management port 9091, which serves control routes without authentication (row 1.149). In chart 5.0.30 and Operator v1.3.11 the port is carried on the same Service as the client gRPC port, with a ClusterIP default, so changing that Service's type to reach the client port also puts 9091 in its port list. The chart's `metrics.enabled: false` removes the Service port but not the listener, and the health probes use 9091; the Operator's Service code adds the port with no field to remove it. This is a reading of the cited chart and Operator files at those pins, not a cluster test, and other chart templates and the Operator's generated CRD were not checked. Two components and their sources are added (#452).
- realtime-voice-infra.md: document coturn's hashed-key alternative to a clear TURN password in `turnserver.conf` (row 1.139). At coturn 4.18.0 a static user may be written `user=NAME:0xKEY`; the key is an unsalted MD5 of `user:realm:password`, so it is bound to the user and realm, a weak password remains guessable offline, and the key must be protected as a credential. The guide also records, from a trace of the pinned source, that `turnadmin -k` takes the password only as the `-p` argument and does not erase it, and that this conclusion is limited to the files reviewed. No key-generation command is given, because the only vendor procedure puts the password on a command line. Five coturn 4.18.0 sources are added (#451).
- self-hosted-ci-runners.md: the offline `config.toml` inspection in Verify was run on 20 synthetic fixtures with Python 3.14.4, and its record changes from reasoned to demonstrated for file inspection only. The reading of its output is corrected: `null` means absent from the inspected table only, an unset `services_privileged` inherits `privileged`, the autoscaler limits must be the integer `1` and anything else there is flagged, other unexpected types, errors and partial output are inconclusive, and the runner `token` key is omitted but reported fields are not redacted. The Verify prose now also says how to read the Kubernetes keys the block prints. What counts as a finding, and everything about a live runner or daemon, stays reasoned.
