(#365)
- LiteFS HTTP API exposure (#364), bind-audit record 72, row 1.153. `sqlite.md` section 6 traces v0.5.14's unauthenticated cleartext API: the server accepts HTTP/1 and h2c, and LiteFS's client uses h2c only. The default and example `http.addr` are `:20202`, listening on every interface; an explicitly empty address also binds every interface on a system-chosen port. The address comes from the configuration file, with default search paths or `-config PATH`, no direct address flag, and environment expansion unless `-no-expand-env`. The route table states role and database conditions: `/import` and HTTP/2 POST `/stream` require a primary and reject replicas; export reads an existing local database on either role, and `/tx` can apply valid LTX input to an existing database on either role without checking halt-lock ownership. Stream filtering and snapshots, transaction validation, per-database halt creation and write locks on either role, lock expiration and matching-id release, candidate promotion, and primary handoff with a connected subscriber and a supporting lease are explicit. State and debug routes work on either role; `/debug/rand` joins pprof and expvar in the unconditional debug inventory. Consul advertises each node's own private API URL, while static replicas must configure the primary's URL. Container exposure includes same-network peers, host networking, shared namespaces, routing, firewall, and publication. The separate application proxy requires `proxy.target`, `proxy.db`, and `proxy.addr`; `:8080` is an example. Verify distinguishes a direct LiteFS response from a fronting proxy response and retains role/state qualifications and its REASONED status. Pinned sources include the database methods, stream enumeration and snapshots, and static replica URL semantics; Docker networking sources support the exposure correction. No live listener was run.
- Triton administrative endpoints (#363), bind-audit record 45. `model-servers.md` now names every restriction category, separates unrestricted reads and logging/trace settings from model-control-mode and client-shared-memory opt-ins, and gives HTTP and gRPC restriction syntax covering all categories. It records the file-path mutation refusals and the separate unauthenticated metrics listener. Administrative API sources are pinned to 546a78766fb112128aa0b10a70c55f4f39c3b1df. The new logging authorization probe is REASONED; row 1.152 tracks its live demonstration. No Triton server was run. It names two cloud paths the flags do not cover: the SageMaker multi-model invoke route, and the Vertex AI redirect header, which reaches `metrics` unrestricted.
- Ray per-node agent listeners (#362), bind-audit record 60. With optional dashboard dependencies present, step 2 names default HTTP port 52365 (also for local `ray.init()` clusters) plus OS-assigned agent gRPC, runtime env HTTP and metrics ports, with all four port flags. Minimal mode depends on missing dependencies, not the install command: dashboard agent HTTP/gRPC and reporter/metrics are absent; only runtime env HTTP remains among these four. Metrics collection is enabled by default and disabling it skips the exporter. HTTP binds the effective node IP, adding resolved localhost only outside the exact `127.0.0.1`/`::1`/`localhost` set; gRPC uses resolved localhost for that set, otherwise a wildcard. Metrics use resolved localhost for that set, otherwise all IPv4 interfaces; their AF_INET server cannot bind `::1`, read from source, not run. Runtime env HTTP binds the effective node IP. There is no independent agent bind-host setting; localhost normalization and the whole-node `RAY_ENABLE_WINDOWS_OR_OSX_CLUSTER` setting are explained. `--include-dashboard=false` stops neither agent. The head dashboard forwards to the head agent's job manager; worker agents also accept cluster jobs. Token auth covers agent control APIs except two health paths, but never metrics. Verify adds per-node agent probes and direct `/logs/` token controls, REASONED for lack of an isolated multi-node cluster and tracked in row 2.40; the index maps 52365. All new behavior claims cite pinned sources in `ray.md`; no live Ray behavior was demonstrated.
- Milvus management HTTP exposure (#361), bind-audit record 85, row 1.148. `vector-databases.md` labels 9091 as management/metrics HTTP with an optional WebUI and documents its control routes at v2.6.24 and v3.0.2, none of which checks credentials (log level, component stop, the proxy's garbage-collection and query-node controls and encryption-zone key backup, v3.0.2's read-queue and backfill routes, the mix coordinator's own operational controls and its configuration and WAL changes, pprof on by default, and the event-log listener), their wildcard bind without a bind-address setting, the METRICS_PORT fallback, and the standalone Compose publication. It recommends deleting the 9091 mapping, which the in-container healthcheck does not need, with a loopback-only mapping as the one exception for host administration, states the selected images' older versions, and pins the source evidence to both reviewed commits. Source review only; no live Milvus or container-network test. Verify probes the management listener from a non-peer, with the container's healthcheck and a namespace inventory showing the `milvus` process on the port as the positive control when the mapping is deleted, and row 1.149 tracks the live demonstration and the Helm and Operator exposure.

## 2026-09-25

- Fooocus exposure gaps (#359), row 1.145, which the maintainer has not ruled on. `image-gen-uis.md` pins Fooocus at v2.5.5 and cites each claim at that tag: `--listen` alone binds `0.0.0.0` and the flag's absence `127.0.0.1`, a parse demonstrated in `argparse`; the port is 7865 by default, unless `GRADIO_SERVER_PORT` is set or 7865 is taken; the launch line passes `--listen 127.0.0.1 --port 7865`, because `launch.py` sets `GRADIO_SERVER_PORT` only when it is unset and gradio 3.41.2, given no port, searches upward from that value, so an inherited variable or an occupied port moves the UI; and it starts Fooocus with `launch.py`, because `entry_with_update.py` fetches `origin` and fast-forwards the checked-out branch. A container paragraph says the upstream image, Compose file and `docker.md` examples pass a bare `--listen` and publish `7865:7865`, and prescribes `-p 127.0.0.1:7865:7865` with explicit `CMDARGS`, or `--listen 127.0.0.1` under host networking, and an image built from the pinned checkout (`docker compose up --build`, `pull_policy: build`, or `docker build . -t fooocus`) in place of the GHCR image the upstream Compose file and `docker run` example name; it says that the Compose build is tagged `ghcr.io/lllyasviel/fooocus`, because the service keeps that `image:` name, and the `docker build` one `fooocus`, and gives the uid lookup for each. The `auth.json` text gives where the file goes (the checkout root, or a read-only mount at `/content/app/auth.json`), ownership by the image user's uid at mode `0400`, and `hash` entries, citing `auth_list_to_dict` for how `hash` and `pass` entries are stored and `check_auth` for the login comparison, and states the v2.5.5 loader's results, demonstrated on the loader alone with no server: an unreadable file raises `PermissionError`, and a malformed file or an empty list returns no credentials. The server-level outcomes are read from the source, not observed, and the guide and the exposure index state them as expectations: an unreadable file should stop startup, and a malformed file or an empty list should start the UI with no login. Verify probes 7865, probes every port whatever an earlier probe returned, and says to probe over IPv4 and IPv6; its `ss` expectations cover host processes, and for a container it reads the PORTS column of `docker ps` and the probes from another host instead, because socket visibility varies by Docker version and config. It marks the Fooocus listener entries REASONED, because no listener may be opened on the authoring host; row 1.146 tracks the demonstration of those entries and of the two server-level `auth.json` outcomes. The exposure index gains a 7865 row. From pinned sources (Fooocus v2.5.5, gradio 3.41.2); no Fooocus instance was run. The start block runs only when the checkout's HEAD is the pinned v2.5.5 tag (a failed clone is deleted and cloned again, since the working tree is not checked), the loopback InvokeAI probe bypasses any proxy, and host networking is read with `ss`. The clone and the start are now separate blocks; the two fixed-target probes carry `-g`.
- CONTRIBUTING rule 7: the guarded read turns off errexit too (#358), on the maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-26T00:27Z, "Add +e now". The read exception's first condition is now `set +x +a +e`, run straight after the trap line and before any command whose failure could trigger an inherited errexit, the last command of an `&&` or `||` list included, because under an inherited `set -e` (a script, `bash -e`) the first check that failed as expected ended the subshell silently: on a bash 5.3.9 reproduction of `sqlite.md`'s bundle scan, a clean grep, the result that block looks for, printed nothing, and with the new guard printed its result and went on. A block that needs errexit may turn it back on afterwards, on its own line, and the rule says the guard governs the subshell only and that the subshell's status stops a caller under `set -e` only where errexit applies to the subshell command. All 27 guard lines in 15 guides change: 21 plain guards gain `+e`, with `haproxy.md`'s and `sqlite.md`'s trailing comments kept in column; `code-server.md`'s cookie probe moves its trap line and guard to the top of the subshell, ahead of the unauthenticated request that could end it under `set -e` before the positive control; and the six create-once writers in `chat-uis.md`, `image-gen-uis.md` and `model-servers.md` split `set -eC +x +a` into the guard and a `set -eC` line, keeping errexit there on purpose. `gitops-controllers.md`'s Argo CD comment names errexit. Under `bash -e` with stub tools, `haproxy.md`'s mTLS probes, `nats.md`'s refused publishes and `realtime-voice-infra.md`'s anonymous allocation had each ended their block early, and the code-server and TURN blocks were shown reaching their prompts with the new guard. Three blocks had failure paths that an inherited `set -e` covered only in part, and a plain shell not at all (an openssl failure stopped only with pipefail as well, and an empty output in no mode), so each now guards them explicitly: `kubernetes.md`'s password-file creation runs under `set -o pipefail` and refuses to create the Secret when any part of it fails, so `kubectl create secret` can no longer read a stale `.htpasswd` that `-vi` accepts because it holds the same password; `gitops-controllers.md`'s Flux signature is computed with `pipefail` set inside the command substitution, and a failed or empty result stops the block before it sends `X-Signature: sha256=` as its positive control; and `nats.md`'s publisher refuses a failed or empty `date` marker instead of publishing `marker-`. Each was run in plain bash and under `-e`, `-eu`, `-o pipefail`, `-e -o pipefail` and `-eu -o pipefail`, with stub tools, injected command failures and no service. No gate checks the guard's wording, so none changed. Backlog: row 1.147 (the `/invocations` probe's paste marker and value count), and row 1.105 now requires the coturn fixture's `denied-peer-ip` policy to mirror the deployment's. The plugin bundle and `site/llms-full.txt` are rebuilt.
- Workflow action pins held by a gate (#357), row 3.21. The new `tools/check_workflow_pins.py` fails any `uses:` in `.github/workflows/` or in an `action.yml` that is not a full 40-character lowercase commit SHA followed by `# vX.Y.Z`, so a tag, a branch, an expression, a short or uppercase SHA, or a missing or malformed release comment can no longer merge green. A Docker action must be pinned by `@sha256:` digest, and a local `./` action must resolve to a file the gate also reads. The gate is standard library only and offline. It reads the files line by line and refuses any YAML construct outside a small declared subset (an anchor, alias or tag; a quoted scalar or flow collection that holds `uses` or runs past its line; a double-quoted scalar carrying a backslash inside a flow collection, because an escape such as `"u\x73es"` is `uses` to YAML; a flow collection closed by the wrong kind of bracket; a quoted block key that is not a plain name; a `uses:` value that is not plain and on the key's line; the word `uses` inside a block scalar), and reads past `run: |` blocks by YAML's own indentation rule. It cannot check that a SHA exists or is the commit the named release points at, which needs the network. `tools/test_workflow_pins.py` runs the real gate against 160 cases, among them the `\x`, `\u` and `\U` escape of each distinct letter of `uses` in a step, a reusable-workflow job and a composite action, both inside a flow mapping and as a block key, and each check in the gate has one that fails when the check is removed. The gate's and the suite's wrappers in `run_all_checks.sh` count an exit 0 as a pass only when that script prints its ok line. The workflows are unchanged.
- LobeChat and Stable Diffusion WebUI secrets out of launch argv (#356), the two argv items of row 1.135. `chat-uis.md`: `KEY_VAULTS_SECRET`, `AUTH_SECRET` and the Google OAuth client secret, which the old block also left in argv, now reach the container through `--env-file` (docker/cli v27.5.1) from an owner-only file created once under `umask 077` and `set -C`, refusing an existing target in any form before anything is generated, with the two generated values held in the subshell's positional parameters and the client secret prompted under rule 7's `read` exception; the launch block takes the client ID and allowed emails on a guarded `set --` line, refuses a secrets path that is not a regular file or is a symlink before any `grep` reads it, and, reading the file as text, refuses a file without exactly one line for each of the three secrets or with any other line, since a bare name in an env file is looked up in the CLI's environment; it cannot detect a client secret cut short, and the guide says so. Both guides describe `set -C` as overwrite protection for an existing regular file only, holding only in directories no other account can write to or replace, and chat-uis says a multi-line paste at the hidden prompt leaves its later lines for the shell to run. The guide names `-e NAME` and Compose `env_file` and `secrets` as the other inputs, and says the values stay readable through `docker inspect` to anyone with the Docker socket and through the container process's `/proc/<pid>/environ`. `image-gen-uis.md`: AUTOMATIC1111 (pinned at v1.10.1) takes its login from `--gradio-auth-path`, with the file created the same way; the guide says the launcher's startup line echoes `--gradio-auth` and `--api-auth` passwords in clear text, `COMMANDLINE_ARGS` included, and the launch block clears `COMMANDLINE_ARGS` and `GRADIO_SERVER_NAME` and passes `--server-name 127.0.0.1` explicitly, because without `--listen` or `--server-name` Gradio 3.41.2 binds to an inherited `GRADIO_SERVER_NAME`, and, after the same regular-file check and reading the file as text, refuses a file with an empty or malformed password line or with a user name on two lines, of which Gradio 3.41.2 would keep only the last password; its common-mistakes entry separates a command-line password, readable by other local accounts, from one in `COMMANDLINE_ARGS`, held in the environment for the same account and root. On the maintainer's 2026-09-25 "Disclose + row" ruling, the guide also discloses, marked as read from the code and not run, that `/internal/sysinfo` returns `COMMANDLINE_ARGS` unredacted and appears to need no login, and tells the reader to use the file, keep credentials out of `COMMANDLINE_ARGS` and refuse `/internal/sysinfo` at the proxy. Both keep their loopback binds. In both launch blocks the validator patterns spell out ASCII character sets instead of ranges, whose meaning can depend on the locale, except the LobeChat client-secret pattern, which refuses only control characters as the locale defines them, as the guide says; a count is used only from a `grep` that succeeded; a check that cannot read the file refuses with a message that says it could not check; the duplicate-user check passes only when `awk` exits 0 and prints `ok`, which it does only after reading at least one line; and an inherited `set -e` no longer stops a valid file from reaching the launch. Row 1.142 records `--api-auth`, whose non-argv inputs are untraced and whose parser splits at every `:`, and row 1.143 the live confirmation of the sysinfo finding. It also adds row 1.144. Both guides now say a default ACL overrides `umask 077`, and row 1.141 covers their writers too.
- CSP-hash gate scope (#352), row 3.18 and the maintainer's P5 ruling ("all three"), both of 2026-09-25. `tools/check_csp_hashes.py` now refuses any `.xhtml` or `.xht` under `site/` in any case, because a browser parses XHTML as XML where the gate's page model does not hold, and refuses `.svgz`, which it cannot read as text, and `.gz`, `.br` and `.zst`, which a host serving precompressed files can serve for the uncompressed name. A suffix is read from the last dot of the whole name, so a dotfile named `.svg` or `.xhtml` is classified rather than skipped, and a name ending with a dot is refused. It parses every `.svg` with expat and fails one carrying a `script` or `style` element, an `on*` or `style` attribute, in any case or namespace, XHTML inside `<foreignObject>` included, or a `javascript:` URL in any attribute, an `href` or an animation's `to`, `from`, `by` or `values` alike, matched on the scheme after control characters and spaces are removed; and it fails closed on a file it cannot read, decode as UTF-8 or parse, or one carrying a `<!DOCTYPE>`, a processing instruction, or an encoding or version declaration other than UTF-8 and 1.0. On the HTML pages it now refuses any `on*` attribute, reported with its line and accounted for literally as `style=` already was, with every attribute name in a tag counted whether it carries a value or not; that count has no comment or raw-text state, so it is kept as defense in depth, behind four rules that ask `html.parser` nothing: every valued `on...=` outside the hash-pinned blocks is refused whatever its context, a comment, CDATA section or `<!`/`<?` declaration holding a `<` and a `textarea`, `title`, `xmp`, `noscript`, `plaintext`, `noembed`, `noframes` or `iframe` holding a `<` are refused, and so are any `<math>`, an SVG `title`, `desc`, `foreignObject`, `style` or `script`, and, anywhere on the page including the pinned bodies, a malformed end tag (`</` not followed by a letter), refused conservatively, since in the tokenizer's data state most are bogus comments, which the gate's literal tag reading does not model, though `</>` is dropped, `</` at the end of input is text, and inside script or style data, RCDATA or an attribute value they stay text. Those rules did not close the class (a `<frameset>` or `<select>` around the pinned stylesheet still let a browser build a handler from what `html.parser` hashed as the block), so the rules are now defense in depth behind an element allowlist: every start or end tag name on a page, read context-free over the whole text and compared ASCII case-insensitively, must be one of the 28 elements the real pages use, and extending the list is a reviewed change to the gate. Prose `on` words and `data-onload` pass, and plain text in a comment or `<title>` passes. The walk is over lstat: any symbolic link under `site/` (or `site/` itself), a FIFO or device, and a metadata read or directory listing that fails are findings, which also closes the documented gap that a symlinked directory was never walked. `site/favicon.svg` passes, and the ok line now counts the SVGs inspected. `tools/test_csp_hashes.py` keeps its 74 cases, every run now with the real favicon in place, and adds 426 row 3.18, P5 and review-round cases (376 refusals, 50 passes) and 3 injected I/O failures; dropping any one of tab, LF, CR, space or DEL from the URL noise set turns it red, as does disabling the allowlist or any one of the four refused-context rules (118, 20, 82, 50 and 58 FAIL lines respectively for the allowlist and rules 1 to 4, a count of failed assertions, message checks included, not of distinct failing fixtures). `javascript:` URLs on HTML pages and external references from an SVG are outside both rulings and stated as unchecked in the gate's docstring.
- Model servers: the remaining secrets off launch argv (#355), row 1.137. `model-servers.md`: vLLM's key now comes from an owner-only YAML file through `--config`, after the pinned commits showed `--api-key` and the `VLLM_API_KEY` fallback; the launch block clears `VLLM_API_KEY` and `VLLM_USE_RUST_FRONTEND`, because the opt-in Rust frontend receives the key in `--args-json` on its command line. Triton's restricted-API value is stated to be argv-only at the pinned parser, readable by other accounts, the same account and root for the server's lifetime and possibly kept by command-line auditing after it, with the reverse proxy as the control. text-generation-webui's login moves to a generated `--gradio-auth-path` file and its API keys to a `CMD_FLAGS.txt` that a create-once block writes in a private `--user-data-dir`, refusing a directory that holds a `.git` entry or has one in a physical ancestor (a relative `cd -P ..` walk that builds no path string and runs no git; bind mounts, external work trees and a `.git` created between the check and the write are disclosed as undetected) or one where the file already exists, with the text-generation-webui substitutions single-quoted, counted and behind a paste marker, and with the tracked `user_data/CMD_FLAGS.txt`, the update wizard's `git merge --autostash` and `git reset --hard`, the plaintext startup log of both keys and the one-click launcher's unquoted re-parsing of its arguments disclosed; the guide starts `server.py` directly. llama-server's launch refuses inherited `LLAMA_ARG_*` variables. Verify's `pgrep` pattern widens to `--admin-key`, `--gradio-auth`, `--http-restricted-api` and `--grpc-restricted-protocol`, demonstrated against stub processes with no server run, and names the abbreviated options it misses. It also refuses an existing target of any kind in the vLLM and Gradio writers, after making their directory owner-only, a guarantee stated to hold only in owner-only directories no other account can replace, and adds row 1.141. #350's SGLang and llama.cpp writers get the same directory-first, any-existing-target refusal, and the vLLM, SGLang and TGI launch placeholders and the Verify `/invocations` model placeholder are now single-quoted with a substitute-inside-the-quotes note.
- CONTRIBUTING rule 7: a third exception, for a tool with no input for a secret outside argv (#354), on the maintainer's 2026-09-25 ruling. The clause covers a tool that takes the secret only in argv, as `turnutils_uclient` takes the TURN password only as `-w` (checked at coturn 4.18.0); it never covers curl and relaxes neither the stdin rule nor the environment exception. The guide must cite, at a pinned tag, the source that takes the secret from argv and state, from a trace across the source files the tool's own build target compiles (or of every write to the secret's buffer together with every read or use of the credential), that no stdin, environment, file, argument-file or prompt input exists; any such input is used instead, and a different tool is preferred where one exists. The block prompts under the `read` exception's five conditions, refuses an empty value or a control character, and passes the value as one quoted word, which keeps it out of shell history, scrollback and the `set --` line and nothing more. The guide says the secret is readable in argv by other local accounts, the same account and root for the process's lifetime and may be recorded by command-line auditing, and runs the block only with a throwaway or short-lived credential on a host with no untrusted local account; a long-lived secret that derives credentials, such as the shared secret `-W` takes, never qualifies. The closing gate sentence now says the gate checks neither exception, and the `read` exception's shell-history sentence is now scoped to a secret typed into the pasted text or the `set --` line, so it no longer contradicts the new clause. `DECISIONS.md` records both rulings. No gate changed. It also brings `realtime-voice-infra.md`'s coturn disclosure in line with the clause (the traced files, the alternatives not yet evaluated, the same account, root and command-line auditing) and adds row 1.140.
- Realtime voice: TURN and LiveKit secrets out of argv where the tool allows it (#353), two row 1.135 argv items. `realtime-voice-infra.md` Verify: the LiveKit rotation token is now minted with the API secret prompted under the guarded `read -r -s` exception and handed to `lk` as a guarded one-command `LIVEKIT_API_KEY`/`LIVEKIT_API_SECRET` prefix, never exported, instead of `--api-secret` in argv; livekit-cli is pinned at v2.18.2, which has no stdin input for the pair and uses the environment pair unless `--project` or `--subdomain` is given, and the guide states the `/proc/<pid>/environ` window. `turnutils_uclient` takes its password only through `-w` (checked at coturn 4.18.0), so its credentialed run now prompts for a throwaway fixture user's password, keeping it out of history, says plainly that the password is in that process's argv for the run, and requires the isolated fixture, a host with no untrusted local users, no `-h` (which keeps the client and its argv alive) and never `-W` with a deployment's `static-auth-secret`; the anonymous and credentialed runs share one guarded block that clears inherited traps and states that it assumes a clean shell. The rotation prose no longer points the reader at a JWT tool that takes the secret on its command line. - llama.cpp and SGLang API keys out of launch argv (#350), the `model-servers.md` items of row 1.135. `model-servers.md`: llama-server now reads its keys from an owner-only file through `--api-key-file` (one key per line at the pinned commit), created once with `openssl rand` under `umask 077` and `set -C`, and the launch block clears `LLAMA_API_KEY` and `LLAMA_ARG_API_KEY_FILE` first; SGLang takes both keys from an owner-only YAML file through `--config` (v0.5.20), written from the subshell's positional parameters behind the trap line, because the pinned sources document no stdin or environment input for them. - CI's Python pinned exactly (#351), row 3.22. `checks.yml` now pins Python 3.14.7 (the release "3.14" resolved to on this workflow's 2026-09-25 runs, so nothing changes today) in one `env:` value next to the shellcheck pin, feeds it to setup-python, and asserts before the gate suite that `python3 --version` is exactly that release, so a patch release or runner image change can no longer change which Python the gates run under without a reviewed bump (the image's other tools still float).
- Egress metadata: the IPv6 IMDSv2 token out of argv (#349), a row 1.135 argv item. `egress-metadata.md`'s commented AWS IPv6 probe now sends its session token to curl on stdin (`-H @-`), as the IPv4 line beside it already does, instead of `-H "X-aws-ec2-metadata-token: $T6"` in curl's argv.
- CI hardening (#348), row 3.17, from the retroactive review of #255. The `Checks` and weekly link-check workflows now pin `actions/checkout` (v4.4.0), `actions/setup-python` (v5.6.0) and `lycheeverse/lychee-action` (v2.9.0) to full commit SHAs instead of floating major tags, and check out without persisting the token. The shellcheck version and its SHA-256 are written once, side by side in the job's `env:`, with the digest's source recorded; the install step downloads and extracts under `$RUNNER_TEMP` with cleanup on exit, and a new step asserts, rather than prints, that a bare `shellcheck` resolves to the installed binary and reports the pinned version before the suite runs. The gate's pass line now names the path of the shellcheck that linted, and its docstring, the workflow comment that claimed local and CI agree, `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`, the suite header and this file's preamble now say the same thing: every gate is offline, the `gates` job that runs them is not, and a local run skips rather than fails without shellcheck. Row 3.21 proposes an offline gate that keeps every workflow action pinned. Row 3.22 tracks pinning Python's exact release.
- Frontend frameworks: the bundle secret out of argv (#347), a row 1.135 argv item. `frontend-frameworks.md` Verify: the secret to search the built client output for is now prompted with the guarded `read -r -s` exception and fed to grep as a pattern on stdin (`-f -`), instead of being typed into grep's argv as a literal; grep still prints only file names (`-l`); it now searches only the output directories that exist (so a missing one no longer forces exit 2 and masks both results), refuses when none exists, and reads grep's status separately to report a finding, a clean result or an error. The block clears inherited traps, states that it assumes a clean shell, and the prose names what the prompt does not protect.
- DECISIONS.md backfill (#346), on the maintainer's 2026-09-25 ruling to backfill in a separate pull request. `DECISIONS.md` now records the repository-governing rulings from 2026-09-14 to 2026-09-24 that had lived only in the private record: rule 7's trap scope and hostile-shell limits, its IFS and DEBUG-trap requirements, the no-stdin framing of the environment exception and its prefix-assignment guard, and named-variable keys; the QA stopping rule; the two CONTRIBUTING lines on demonstrated steps; the exposure-index citation pass and "May be" names; six questions closed as overtaken; no listener-opening demonstrations until isolation exists; the advisory pinned-citation gate; rule 5's environment-capability carve-out; row 3.14 and `--strict-guards`; no `Co-Authored-By` trailer in this repository's commits; the C2 guard-conventions enforcement; the credential-in-argv rule and sweep; the Argo Workflows section; and the narrow guard-conventions gate. Each entry records only what the source says.
- Next.js credentials out of argv (#345), the first argv item of row 1.135. `nextjs.md` Verify: the admin and non-admin session cookies are now prompted with the guarded `read -r -s` exception and sent to curl on stdin (`--header @-`), instead of `-b "$COOKIE_NAME=$VALID_COOKIE"` in curl's argv, from variables the reader set in their own shell; the cookie name goes on a guarded `set --` line. The bundle secret scan prompts for the session-signing secret and feeds it to grep as a pattern on stdin (`-f -`), instead of in grep's argv, and prints only matching file names (`-l`), so a finding does not echo the secret. Both blocks clear inherited traps and state that they assume a clean shell, and the prose names the paste hazard without bracketed paste. Drafted by a pool worker and applied by the orchestrator.
- MinIO follow-ups (#344), row 1.136, from the retroactive review of #256. `minio.md`: section 4.1's mapping examples are the default OIDC configuration; a named configuration needs its case-sensitive name appended to each variable, and claim mapping serves only one provider per deployment (AIStor's OpenID settings and OIDC access pages); an identity whose token maps to no policy gains no identity-based access, while an anonymous bucket policy still applies; and section 4.2 says to import the `app` alias before generating a presigned URL with it.
- The 2026-09-25 rulings (#342). `DECISIONS.md` records the maintainer's 2026-09-24 decision declining a row-format gate, and their 2026-09-25 rulings on Flower's unix socket, Verify marking (a corpus convention), the CSP-hash gate's scope, the lychee sweep's strictness, the vendored AIQT files' licence and a retroactive review of #255 and #256, plus a profile correction; P3 and P4 were recorded by #340 and #341. `PENDING-DECISIONS.md` now has nothing open: P1 is struck as deferred to the OPF standard and P2 as covered by the 2026-09-24 decision. `TODO.md` gains rows 1.136 and 3.17 (the review's findings) and 3.18 to 3.20 (the ruled tooling work), and row 1.135 moves to Priority 1.
- The vendored AIQT files' licence (#343), on the maintainer's 2026-09-25 rulings. The earlier pin (upstream 8ab5b9a, 2026-09-09) predated AIQT Guardrails' move to the Apache License 2.0, so the vendored files are re-pinned to upstream ad60d25 (pack 1.0.5): six of the nine change here, and the two `AIQT_SITE_HOST` local patches are now upstream and dropped. One new local patch lets `tools/check_newtab.py` take its coverage roots from `AIQT_NEWTAB_ROOTS`, because upstream also requires an `opf/site/` tree that exists only there; the gate suite sets it to `site`. `.aiqt/` now carries the pack's `LICENSE` and `NOTICE`, byte-identical to upstream, and `.aiqt/PIN` records the vendored and adapted material. README, CONTRIBUTING, SECURITY and CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md keep CC0 for everything else and name that material as the Apache-2.0 exception.
- Container ports cited in the exposure index (#341), carrying the maintainer's P4 ruling (option A). A container port behind a remapped publication is now cited like any other mention rather than allowlisted: the 9001 row cites `ai-infra-services.md` for the Onyx development Compose MinIO console, and the 5432 and 8123 rows cite `llm-observability.md` for Helicone's PostgreSQL and ClickHouse; the 8123 row's credential cell now separates ClickHouse's base default from what the guide says of Helicone's Compose, matching the 18123 row, and the 9001 row labels its credential text as MinIO's. P4 moves from `PENDING-DECISIONS.md` to `DECISIONS.md`. The three allowlist entries are gone, and the allowlist no longer names that reason.
- Trap clearing in positional-parameter blocks (#340), row 1.134, carrying the maintainer's P3 ruling. The 22 blocks that bring a secret into a subshell's positional parameters, on a `set --` line or a later one that captures a command substitution, with no `read`, now clear inherited DEBUG, RETURN and ERR traps before the secret enters, as the guarded-read blocks already did (#310); the one in `supabase-self-hosted.md` holds the public anon JWT and is cleared for consistency. CONTRIBUTING's rule 7 now requires this of every block that brings a secret into its subshell, and its fifth `read` condition names the command. The two `ai-infra-services.md` credential blocks now send the header to curl on stdin (`--header @-`) instead of in argv, and refuse a header containing a control character. Row 1.135 records the secret-bearing blocks a trap line cannot fix. P3 moves from `PENDING-DECISIONS.md` to `DECISIONS.md`, with the same day's ruling on this pull request's scope.
- Flower unix socket (#339). `workflow-orchestrators.md`: with `--unix_socket` set to a filesystem path, Flower v2.2.0 gives the socket file a fixed mode of `0o777` at each start, recreating the socket, so on Linux the socket's directory is what limits which unprivileged accounts can connect. Put the socket in a directory of its own, owned by Flower's user, with a proxy-only group, mode `0750` and no ACL entry that grants others search; set those in whatever creates the directory (files under `/run` are cleared at boot, and systemd's `RuntimeDirectoryMode=` defaults to `0755`); give the proxy the directory's group as the kernel sees it, after any user-namespace or idmapped-mount mapping; and never use an abstract socket name, which no file permission protects. A new Verify step checks the directory with `stat` and `getfacl`: its `stat` mode and named-ACL checks were demonstrated on the authoring host (a named ACL entry hidden from `stat`, and a masked entry restored by `chmod`), while its socket line is reasoned and row 2.34 tracks it. From pinned sources (Flower v2.2.0, Tornado v6.5.10, systemd v257, Docker's moby docker-v29.8.1), FHS 3.0, the kernel's idmappings documentation, and the Linux `unix(7)`, `path_resolution(7)`, `user_namespaces(7)`, `acl(5)`, `getfacl(1)` and `stat(1)` manual pages; no live socket was run.
- Weaviate follow-ups (#338), from the #323 review. `vector-databases.md`: the environment step re-applies the gRPC and Raft port defaults when their variable is unset or empty, and the gossip and cluster-API defaults only when unset (an empty value stops startup with a parse error). From pinned sources (v1.39.6).
- Milvus wording (#337), from the #336 review. `vector-databases.md`: the same-machine exception is attributed to a Kubernetes NetworkPolicy, as its source says, and Milvus peers are admitted by pod selector in a network policy or by address in a host firewall.
- Milvus follow-ups (#336), from the #335 review. `vector-databases.md`: publishing 19530 follows section 1 (to loopback, or behind a reverse proxy, tunnel or tailnet), and the firewall advice is default-deny network policy or a default-deny host firewall that refuses other hosts on the random fallback ports too, admitting Milvus peers by source address; and, because neither control reliably stops processes on the same machine (they can reach a native install over loopback and a container or pod at its own address, and Kubernetes always allows the pod's own node), every host running a Milvus component, Kubernetes nodes included, should carry no untrusted workloads or users; the internal-port text still advises against host networking and a shared network namespace. The exposure index's 19529 row and its 21123, 21124, 22125, 22222 row now carry the one-way, off-by-default and plaintext-fallback (certificate or key) conditions for internal TLS, and the 21123, 21124, 22125, 22222 row also carries the random-port fallback.
- Milvus internal ports (#335). `vector-databases.md`: in v3.0.2 and v2.6.24 the proxy's internal port (19529), query node (21123), data node (21124), mix coordinator (22125) and streaming node (22222) bind every interface (each `ip` setting is only the advertised address); their interceptors check only routing metadata, so these internal APIs, including the query node's `Search` and `Query`, check no credentials, and internal TLS is one-way, off by default, and falls back to plaintext if its certificate does not load; the query node, data node and streaming node fall back to a random port, still on every interface, when theirs is taken. A standalone Milvus runs all five; publish only 19530, give Milvus its own network, and confine the rest with network policy or a host firewall that matches the Milvus processes rather than only these port numbers. The port table and two new exposure-index rows cover the ports. From pinned sources and Milvus's TLS documentation; no live bind was run.
- Carry-forwards (#334). `mongodb.md`'s keyfile tutorial Sources entry now quotes its statement that internal authentication also enforces user access control; `model-servers.md`'s TGI Sources label says the image built from the repository's main `Dockerfile`, matching the body; the exposure index's 25672 row names `RABBITMQ_NODE_PORT` plus 20000 and the settings that replace it, matching `rabbitmq.md`.
- Carry-forwards (#333). `caddy.md`: the official Docker runtime images EXPOSE 2019 but keep the admin API on the loopback of the container's network namespace, which is the host's loopback under host networking and is shared with pod or `--network container:` peers; do not widen `admin` to use that port. `elasticsearch.md`: the Verify text expects wildcard listeners inside an official container on a bridge or user-defined network unless overridden, checks that 9200 reaches only intended clients and 9300 only cluster peers, and treats a wildcard under host networking as a finding, and the image Sources line names both `-E` pass-through forms. From pinned sources.
- Elasticsearch official images (#332). `elasticsearch.md`: the official images (8.19.22, 9.4.6 and 9.5.3) ship an `elasticsearch.yml` with `network.host: 0.0.0.0`, so unless an environment variable, a `-E` setting after `eswrapper` or `elasticsearch` in the command or a bind-mounted configuration overrides it, transport (9300) listens on all interfaces as well as HTTP; the exposure index's 9300 row says so. Also, from the #331 review, `nats.md` states that the relative configuration path loads only from the directory the image copies it to (`/` on scratch, `C:\` on Windows) and cites the bundled configuration in all eight published directories. From pinned sources and Elastic's documentation.
- NATS image follow-ups (#331), from the #330 review. `nats.md`: the image paragraph adds the default working directory to its conditions, because the scratch and Windows commands name the configuration by a relative path, and Sources now cite the server release each image carries (2.15.0 and 2.14.7). The exposure index's 8222 row says `0.0.0.0` instead of "every address". From pinned sources.
- NATS official images (#330). `nats.md`: every published `nats` tag (2.14 and 2.15; scratch, Alpine, Nano Server and Windows Server Core), run with its default command and no configuration mounted over the bundled one, loads a bundled `nats-server.conf` with client port 4222 and no client authorization, `monitor_port: 8222`, and a cluster listener on 6222 whose route credentials (`ruser`, `T0pS3cr3t`) and cluster name are published in the image source; none sets a host, so each listens on `0.0.0.0`, which Go opens as a dual-stack socket where the host supports IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses, and arguments replace the command, so the bundled configuration is not loaded unless they name it again with `--config` or `-c`. The monitoring paragraph now notes the images enable it, and the exposure index's 4222, 6222 and 8222 rows say so. Also, from the #329 review, `mosquitto.md` says anything that can reach the container on 9883 (another container on its network, a published port, or host networking) gets the API without a login, and cites the listener default that lets the global `allow_anonymous` apply and the API's JSON and plain-text handlers. From pinned sources; no live bind was run.
- Mosquitto official images (#329). `mosquitto.md`: the images tagged `latest`, `2`, `alpine`, `2.1-alpine` and `2.1.2-alpine` (Mosquitto 2.1.2) ship their own `mosquitto.conf` with `listener 1883` and `allow_anonymous true`, plus `listener 9883` with `protocol http_api`, which serves the dashboard and a read-only HTTP API of broker statistics and listener details; neither listener names an address, and as shipped, with no password file, authentication plugin or ACL, both serve clients that give no credentials. The `1.6-openssl` tags ship upstream's all-commented configuration, so 1.6's default applies: an anonymous listener on 1883 with no address. The 2.0 tags keep 2.0's loopback-only default. The exposure index's 1883 row now says so, and a new 9883 row covers the HTTP API. Also, from the #328 review, `mysql.md` names the MariaDB `MARIADB_` precedence for an empty `_FILE` and says "the matching bind address". From pinned sources; no live bind was run.
- MySQL and MariaDB official images (#328). `mysql.md`: the official MySQL images (8.4, 9.7 and innovation) install a package whose `my.cnf` sets no bind address, so both bind addresses keep their `*` default and 3306 and 33060 listen on every address unless an argument or option file sets a bind address or `skip_networking` (or, for 33060, `mysqlx=OFF`); the official MariaDB images (10.6 through 13.1) comment out the Ubuntu package's `bind-address = 127.0.0.1`, and the UBI package leaves it commented, so they listen on the IPv4 wildcard, and the IPv6 one where available, unless an argument or option file sets a bind address or `skip_networking`. On a new data directory both entrypoints default the root host to `%` when no value is supplied (a `_FILE` naming an empty file leaves it empty) and, for any non-empty root host other than `localhost`, create that root account with `GRANT ALL ON *.* ... WITH GRANT OPTION` (MariaDB also grants `PROXY`); with no root password, hash or random password requested, any non-empty empty-password variable, even `no`, makes that account passwordless. The exposure index's 3306 and 33060 rows now say so, and the X Plugin paragraph now covers 9.7 and 26.7. Also, from the #327 review, `mongodb.md` now says the Docker images "do not start from those settings" and that IPv6 follows `net.ipv6` being true, in the body, Sources and the 27017 row. From pinned sources and the MySQL and MariaDB reference manuals; no live bind was run.
- MongoDB and PostgreSQL official images (#327). `mongodb.md`: on Linux, for their default `mongod` command, the official images (7.0, 8.0 and 8.3) add `--bind_ip_all` when neither the arguments nor a `--config` file sets a bind address, and add `--auth` only when both `MONGO_INITDB_ROOT_*` credentials are set (one alone makes the entrypoint exit), so a container with neither, and with no bind, authorization or internal-authentication setting of its own, listens on every IPv4 address (IPv6 too only when `net.ipv6` is set) with authorization off; the Windows images run `mongod --bind_ip_all` with no such entrypoint; and a mounted configuration takes effect only with `--config` or `-f`, and the entrypoint looks for a bind only in a `--config` file. `postgresql.md`: the official images (14 through 19) initialize a new data directory with `listen_addresses = '*'`, initdb's default `trust` for local and loopback connections (unless `POSTGRES_INITDB_ARGS` sets their methods), and an appended `host all all all` rule using `POSTGRES_HOST_AUTH_METHOD` (by default the `password_encryption` method), so by default loopback inside the container's network namespace needs no password while other addresses get the `POSTGRES_HOST_AUTH_METHOD` method, and `trust` admits every address. The exposure index's 27017 and 5432 rows now say so. Also, from the #326 review, `redis.md`'s image Sources line now cites the Alpine 8.10.2 `CMD` and the Debian and Alpine entrypoints. From pinned sources; no live bind was run.
- Redis official images turn protected mode off (#326). `redis.md` described protected mode confining an unconfigured Redis to loopback while the `default` user is `nopass`; the official Docker images do not keep that default. Their build, from redis/docker-library-redis, patches Redis so `protected-mode` defaults to `no` (every image on the default branch at the pinned commit, 6.2 through 7.4, and the 8.10.2 release tag, the only 8.x build checked), and runs `redis-server` with no configuration file, so in the 7.4.8 and 8.10.2 builds a container started without a configuration file or arguments of its own binds every IPv4 address (and IPv6 where available) and gives the `nopass`, `+@all` `default` user to anyone who reaches it. Section 1 and the exposure index's 6379 row now say so, from pinned source. No live bind was run.
- Default-bind sources, batch 14 (#325). `rabbitmq.md`'s listener port defaults now cite pinned source and name the release (v4.3.6): the management plugin starts a plain HTTP listener on 15672 whenever no management listener is configured; the Erlang distribution port defaults to `RABBITMQ_NODE_PORT` plus 20000, which the environment (a non-empty `RABBITMQ_DIST_PORT` or `DIST_PORT`) or the configuration (`distribution.listener.port_range`) can replace; and the CLI range runs from `RABBITMQ_CTL_DIST_PORT_MIN` (35672) to ten above it unless `RABBITMQ_CTL_DIST_PORT_MAX` is set (an empty value counts as unset, and the unprefixed `CTL_DIST_PORT_*` variable then applies). The all-interfaces claim for the management listener stays on the vendor documentation. Found along the way: the official Docker image sets `loopback_users.guest = false` in `/etc/rabbitmq/conf.d/10-defaults.conf`, so in its containers as shipped (a later configuration layer can set it back) the default `guest`/`guest` administrator, wherever it exists, can log in remotely; the guide's opening, its guest advice and the exposure index's three RabbitMQ credential cells now say so. No live bind was run.
- Default-bind sources, batch 13 (#324). `bi-dashboards.md` now gives, from pinned source, the default binds behind its first-run admin warnings: Metabase v0.63.18 binds a wildcard address, because the jar leaves Jetty's host unset unless the last of a `.lein-env` file in the working directory, a `.boot-env` classpath resource, the environment and JVM options (environ reads them in that order, each overriding the one before, empty values included) to set `MB_JETTY_HOST` gives it a non-blank value, and the official image's entrypoint instead exports `MB_JETTY_HOST=0.0.0.0` (every IPv4 address) when it is unset or empty, on port 3000 unless the last layer to set `MB_JETTY_PORT` gives it a non-blank value; Redash v26.9.0's `server` command, the image default, binds `REDASH_GUNICORN_BIND`, or `[::]:5000` when that is unset or empty. No live bind was run.
- Default-bind sources, batch 12 (#323). Weaviate follow-ups from #322's review, verified at v1.39.6. The Weaviate bullet in `vector-databases.md` now states the precedence once (config file, then environment variables, then flags; every port and settable address it gives is a default, but the environment step re-applies the default for the gRPC, Raft, gossip and cluster-API ports whenever their variable is unset, so a port set only in the config file does not take effect for those, and the gRPC, debug, Prometheus and cluster-API listeners have no address setting and bind every local address whenever they run), and adds that the REST listener's default scheme is `https`, which needs a certificate and key or Weaviate exits at startup, and with them binds `--tls-host` (falling back to `--host`) on a random port, while `--host`/`--port` apply with `--scheme http`, as the official image passes; and that even a single node opens memberlist gossip on 7946 (TCP and UDP) at `CLUSTER_BIND_ADDR`, or every local address when that is unset or empty, the cluster API on 7947 (the gossip port plus one by default) on every local address, and Raft on 8300 with its RPC on 8301 at a non-empty `CLUSTER_BIND_ADDR`, else a non-empty `CLUSTER_ADVERTISE_ADDR`, else memberlist's private advertise address. The exposure index's 7946, 8300 and 8301 rows cite the guide, and new 7946/UDP and 7947 rows do too. No live bind was run.
- Default-bind sources, batch 11 (#322). `vector-databases.md` now gives Weaviate v1.39.6's binds from pinned source: the REST listener takes `--host` (variable `HOST`, whose `localhost` default applies only while `HOST` is unset, since an empty `HOST` binds every local address) and `--port` (variable `PORT`, random when neither is set), and the official `semitechnologies/weaviate` image passes `--host 0.0.0.0 --port 8080`; the gRPC listener always starts, has no host setting and listens on `GRPC_PORT` (default 50051) on every local address whatever `--host` says; the debug listener (pprof and fgprof) binds every local address on 6060 by default unless `GO_PROFILING_DISABLE` is set to a true value, answering 404 until debug endpoints are enabled, and the Prometheus listener binds every local address on 2112 by default when monitoring is enabled. The exposure index's 50051 and 6060 rows say so, and a new 2112 row cites the guide. No live bind was run.

- TGI's official image port (#316). `model-servers.md` said a bare TGI container answers on every interface after giving the launcher default `0.0.0.0:3000`; the image built from its main `Dockerfile` sets `PORT=80`, so the container listens on port 80, which the guide's `ss` listener list now includes. - Wording follow-ups from the #307, #308 and #310 reviews (#312). CONTRIBUTING rule 7's clean-shell assumption now says an inherited function matters when it is named like a command the block calls, and an alias when it is on a word in command position, rather than on any word. The 9004, 9005, 9010 row's credential cell names ClickHouse, so a reader who finds Onyx's MinIO there does not read ClickHouse's empty-password default as MinIO's. `fronting-auth.md` says Authelia passes the host-and-port `:9091`, with its empty host, to `net.Listen`. DONE 1.129 says which of its two Sources lines is pinned.
- Exposure-index gate: backticked pairs and per-guide citations (#311), closing row 1.128.
  - `tools/check_exposure_index.py` now reads a code span (found by a one-line scan with CommonMark's backtick and escape rules: an escaped backtick is literal, a run closes on the next run of the same length, one padding space is stripped; links, raw HTML, autolinks, line breaks and table cells are not modelled) that holds only a published `[host:]H:C` pair, such as `8888:8080` or `127.0.0.1:8888:8080`, in prose or a table cell, and counts both sides. The bind host must be an IPv4 or bracketed IPv6 address, as in the published-mapping shape. A pair whose two sides both have two digits counts only when they are equal, so `80:80` is a pair and `10:30` or `70:30` is not.
  - A row now maps its ports only for the guides it cites. A mention of a port that the index lists singly or in a range of at most 101 ports fails as uncited, unless allowlisted, when no row listing that port cites the guide. A range wider than 101 ports still reports such a mention as unmapped.
  - Run over `main`, the new gate found 20 uncited pairs: 15 the earlier detector already saw, and 5 from backticked pairs. Three are now cited: `agent-builders.md` on row 5432 (Langflow's example Compose publishes `5432:5432`), `llm-observability.md` on row 9000 (the guide lists Helicone's host port `9000` among its Compose publications), and `tunnels.md` on row 3000 (the local backend in its `ssh -R` examples, as the row already cites `cloudflare.md` and `tailscale.md` for their tunnelled `localhost:3000` backends). The other 17 are allowlisted with a reason: outbound destinations, illustrative numbers, test-rig listeners, the README's certificate pointer, and container ports behind remapped publications.
  - Rows 6379 and 9001 also cite `llm-observability.md`, whose Helicone Sources line lists those host ports as bare numbers, which the gate does not read.
  - Row 3000 no longer states a guide count. Its "23 guides" figure was stale, and no gate keeps a count in step.
  - `tools/test_exposure_index.py` gains 19 cases (D42 to D47, N14 to N19, C1 to C7), and M2, M3 and M7 now mention ports from a guide their row cites.
- Rule 7 trap and IFS conditions (#310), row 1.133, carrying the maintainer's rulings of 2026-09-24 at 23:39Z and 23:56Z. CONTRIBUTING rule 7's guarded-read exception now has five conditions: the readonly-`IFS` refusal (already in every block since #301, #303 and #306) and `trap - DEBUG RETURN ERR` before `set +x +a`, ahead of the read, are added. It also states the remaining assumption, a calling shell not altered to intercept the block, with examples (an inherited DEBUG trap still runs once before the `trap -` line and under `extdebug` can skip it; functions, aliases on any word including reserved words, `hash -p` entries and disabled builtins), and says that no command typed into such a shell reliably escapes it, so such blocks belong in a freshly started shell whose startup files the reader trusts. `search-engines.md`'s assumption list is updated to match. Row 1.134 asks whether blocks that hold a secret only in positional parameters, with no read, should clear traps too. All 16 guarded-read blocks now clear the traps, with a one-line comment naming the clean-shell assumption and pointing to rule 7. With stub tools, before this change an inherited DEBUG trap under `set -T` read the typed secret in all 16 blocks, and an ERR trap under `set -E` in 13; after it, in none.
- `surrealdb.md` and `kubernetes.md` guard follow-ups (#309), rows 1.131 and 1.132.
  - `surrealdb.md`: the initial root-password block clears `SURREAL_USER` and `SURREAL_PASS` itself and refuses to start while any other `SURREAL_*` variable is set in the calling shell, naming each, so an inherited `SURREAL_UNAUTHENTICATED`, `SURREAL_BIND` or similar option cannot change the start; with stubs, the old block started with an exported `SURREAL_UNAUTHENTICATED` or `SURREAL_LOG` in the shell, and the new one refuses, naming the variable.
  - `kubernetes.md`: the htpasswd block asks for the password twice and refuses a mismatch or an empty entry, and creates the secret only if `htpasswd -vi` succeeds; its paste warning now describes the two prompts; with stubs, the old block wrote an empty password and ran `kubectl` after a failed verify, and the new one refuses both.
  - Row 1.133 records inherited DEBUG, ERR and RETURN traps (under `set -T` or `set -E`) as a corpus-wide gap in the guarded-read blocks.
- Exposure-index Onyx rows (#308), row 1.127. `ai-infra-services.md` documents Onyx's development Compose publishing PostgreSQL `5432`, Redis `6379`, an inference server `9000`, MinIO `9004` and `9005`, and OpenSearch `9200` on every host interface. Those five index rows now cite it and name the Onyx service in their "May be" cells. The #300 pass found its pairs with the detector's `mentions()`, which does not see these: `ai-infra-services.md:20` gives them as backticked `host:container` pairs, the blind spot row 1.128 records, and `:239` lists bare numbers, which it excludes by design. The gate itself would not flag them either: for a single port or a range of at most 101 ports, it checks only that a mentioned port has a row, not which guides that row cites (only a wider range maps its ports per citing guide); row 1.128 now records that too.
- Environment-borne secrets under rule 7's prefix exception (#306), plan A4, closing row 1.126. The last sites in row 1.126's inventory that exported a secret, or read one without the guarded-read conditions, now follow rule 7.
  - `nats.md` (four blocks): natscli v0.4.0 has no stdin input for the password, so each `nats` command that needs it gets a one-command `NATS_PASSWORD="$pw"` prefix instead of an export. `NATS_USER` stays exported. The blocks turn off allexport as well as tracing, clear every variable they use (`pw`, `srv`, `opts`, `marker`, `v`, `f` and the `NATS_*` names) with `unset -n` then `unset -v` before assigning any, refuse a readonly `IFS` before their quoted ambient-credential loop, and clear each ambient `NATS_*` name the same way. With stub `nats` commands, the old blocks, under inherited shell state, sent an altered password (readonly `IFS=f`), exported `pw` to every `nats` process (`set -a`), sent no password while exporting it under another name (a `NATS_PASSWORD` nameref), and let an ambient `NATS_TOKEN` reach every call, including the no-credentials control (readonly `v`, or an inherited `IFS=_` splitting the unquoted loop). Review found further cases, now covered: a nameref `srv`, `opts`, `marker` or `NATS_USER` pointing at `pw` put the password in argv or replaced it, and a `RANDOM` nameref put it in the V3 publisher's marker, printed and published; that block no longer reads `$RANDOM`, since `date +%s%N` already makes the marker unique. The new blocks refuse or behave as the baseline in each case. The V2 `password mismatch` check is removed: with no exported variable it had nothing to compare, and the guard now covers the cases it caught. The probe order, the wrong-password control and all other messages are unchanged.
  - `nomad-consul.md`: the inline `read -rs NOMAD_TOKEN && export NOMAD_TOKEN` example becomes a guarded block that reads the token from the terminal and runs `NOMAD_TOKEN="$tok" nomad acl policy info anonymous`. The recorded `404` is now labelled as observed with the earlier export form.
  - `surrealdb.md`: the initial root-password block no longer exports `SURREAL_PASS`. It reads into `pw` and runs `SURREAL_USER=root SURREAL_PASS="$pw" surreal start ...`, guarded, and the guide says the password stays readable through `/proc/<pid>/environ` for the server's whole lifetime.
  - `kubernetes.md`: the htpasswd block runs in a subshell with `set +x +a`, both guards and `IFS= read -r -s` from `/dev/tty`; the password still reaches `htpasswd` on stdin. Its paste warning now says what pty tests showed: the block's own lines are read before the prompt; a line pasted after the closing `)` becomes the password without bracketed paste, and runs as a command after the block with it.
  - Sources: new pinned lines for nomad v2.0.7 token input and surreal v3.2.4 `--pass`. Rows 1.131 and 1.132 record two out-of-scope findings.
- Two citation follow-ups (#307), rows 1.129 and 1.130. `fronting-auth.md` now quotes Authelia's default as `tcp://:9091/`, as its pinned Sources line shows, and cites at v4.39.28 how that empty host reaches Go's `net.Listen`, whose documentation says an empty host listens on every local address. `tunnels.md` now scopes "listens on `proxyBindAddr`" to the frp proxy types that open a listener (tcp, udp, http, https and tcpmux), and cites at v0.71.0 the tcpmux listener and the in-process visitor listener and NAT-hole path by which stcp, sudp and xtcp open none.

Row 1.130 records a scope note from #304's review.
- Readonly-IFS guard for guarded reads (#303), plan A1b of row 1.126. A readonly `IFS` inherited from the calling shell survives the `IFS= read` prefix: bash reports the error and reads anyway, so the secret can be altered as it is read (under `readonly IFS=f`, a trailing `f` is dropped). #301 guarded `sqlite.md` against this; the same guard now precedes the read in the seven other guarded-read blocks: `code-server.md` (1), `gitops-controllers.md` (2), `haproxy.md` (2), `surrealdb.md` (1) and `search-engines.md`'s model block (1).
  - Each block runs `{ unset -n IFS; } 2>/dev/null` and exits 2 if it fails, as it does on a readonly `IFS`; it also removes an `IFS` nameref. The read's own `IFS=` assignment still supplies the empty `IFS`.
  - Nothing else in the blocks changed. Row 1.126 still tracks the rest: `surrealdb.md`'s initial root-password read, `nats.md`'s four prompts, `kubernetes.md`'s prompt and `nomad-consul.md`'s example.
  - Two backlog rows record what #300's review found out of scope: row 1.127 (Onyx development backing-service ports the index does not cite) and row 1.128 (the exposure-index detector does not see a backticked `host:container` pair in prose or a table cell, and row 3000's "23 guides" count is stale). Row 1.129 records #302's: `fronting-auth.md` quotes Authelia's default without the trailing `/` its Sources line shows, and cites nothing for how an empty host binds.
- frps proxy-listener citation (#304), row 1.125. `tunnels.md` said a registered proxy binds on `proxyBindAddr` but cited only how that value defaults. Three Sources lines now cite, at pinned tag v0.71.0, the TCP and UDP proxy listeners and the HTTP and HTTPS vhost listeners that bind it. The claim now carries "as of v0.71.0", the frps part of row 1.124.
- `CONTRIBUTING.md` (#299), carrying four further maintainer rulings of 2026-09-24.
  - Rule 7 now allows a second, narrow exception, for a tool that takes a secret through its environment and offers no stdin input for it, such as `nomad` (`NOMAD_TOKEN`, checked at v2.0.7) or `surreal start` (`SURREAL_PASS`, checked at v3.2.4). The tool may have other inputs outside argv, such as a file; the rule does not prefer them. Such a tool may receive the secret as a one-command prefix assignment, never through `export`; stdin stays required wherever the tool offers it. A further ruling makes a guard mandatory: before the prefix assignment, the block clears the name with `unset -n` then `unset -v` and refuses with exit 2 if either fails, because bash still runs a prefixed command whose name is readonly, and the tool then receives the reader's own exported value. The rule states that the value can remain readable through `/proc/<pid>/environ` by the same user and by root while the tool, or any process that inherited it, runs; unsetting the variable alone does not remove it there, though overwriting that memory or replacing the environment, as an `exec` does, can. It therefore counts as exposed for as long as the tool or any process that inherited it runs, which for a server such as `surreal start` is its whole lifetime.
  - Rule 5 now says "Adding a Verify run", the ruling's wording, where #296 had "Adding a demonstration run".
- Default-bind version notes, #293 set (#302), row 1.124. CONTRIBUTING rule 1 asks for a version note on a default that shifts, so the fourteen default-bind claims #293 checked now carry one in the body text, scoped to the part of the sentence the cited source shows.
  - Thirteen name the release of the guide's own pinned citation: oauth2-proxy v7.15.4 and Authelia v4.39.28 (`fronting-auth.md`), Grafana v13.2.2 and Prometheus v3.14.0 (`admin-uis.md`), Phoenix 20.16.0 (`llm-observability.md`), Python 3.14.4 (`python.md`), the Caddy admin API at v2.11.4 (`caddy.md`), Gitea v1.27.3 (`devops-uis.md`), Next.js v16.3.6 (`nextjs.md`), n8n 2.40.6 (`n8n.md`), SvelteKit adapter-node 5.5.7 and Vite 8.3.1 (`frontend-frameworks.md`), and LiteLLM v1.102.1 (`litellm.md`).
  - Docker's publishing default (`docker.md`) is cited from the vendor documentation rather than a tag, so it says "at the time of writing".
  - Only wording changed; no default, command or citation did. Row 1.124 stays open for the #294 set.
- Guarded secret reads, batch 2 (#301), plan A2: `sqlite.md`'s two secret inputs now meet rule 7's guarded-read exception.
  - Bundle scan: the block turns off allexport as well as tracing (`set +x +a`) and clears `tok` with `unset -n` then `unset -v` before its other guards, refusing with exit 2 if either fails, as it does for a readonly name. It also removes any `IFS` nameref with `unset -n` and refuses with exit 2 on a readonly `IFS`, which could otherwise strip or alter the token as it is read; the read's own `IFS=` assignment then gives it an empty `IFS`. The Turso check does the same. Its token-literal and jwt-shape lines and its not-scanning refusals are otherwise unchanged.
  - Turso check, row 1.123: the block no longer reads the token from `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN`. It prompts for it with input hidden, into one subshell-local variable under the same guards, before either request is sent, and refuses an empty, `REPLACE_WITH_` or control-character token, so the token cannot inject a second header. The anonymous and authorized requests are unchanged, and the check stays REASONED (row 2.37).
  - Row 1.126 stays open for the remaining sites.
- Exposure-index citations (#300), a one-off pass by the maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-24. The gate's own detector found 62 guide/port pairs where a guide mentions a port that `exposure-index.md` maps but that port's row does not cite the guide. Each mention was read. The 46 that document a listener, a published port or an exposure on that port now cite their guide. One more pair came from outside the detector: `llm-observability.md` names Langfuse's bundled MinIO S3 API on host port `9090`, but a hard line wrap at `llm-observability.md:34-35` separates the number from its "port" word, a documented blind spot of the gate's `mentions()`; it was read and is cited on the same terms. That makes 47 guide/port pairs, carried by 41 new citations (six guides document both 80 and 443, which share one row), across 16 rows: 22, 53, 80 and 443, 3000, 3001, 5000, 5432, 7860, 8000, 8080, 8081, 8088, 8443, 8888, 9001 and 9090. - Download-probe guard (#297), row 1.122. `sqlite.md`'s download probe named a literal `app.example.com`, so an unsubstituted paste probed that host. It now takes the HTTPS origin and a static path prefix on a `set -- PASTE_WHOLE_BLOCK` line inside a subshell, as the guide's listener and Turso blocks do, and drives its probes from positional parameters rather than named loop variables. It refuses to probe on a missing marker, a wrong value count, an empty or `REPLACE_WITH_` value, a non-HTTPS origin, an origin other than `https://` with a host and an optional numeric `:port` (one trailing `/` is dropped), or a prefix without a leading `/` or containing `//`, `?`, `#`, whitespace or a control character. The probe stays REASONED (row 2.37).
- Guarded secret reads, batch 1 (#298): six `read -s` prompts in four guides now meet rule 7's guarded-read exception.
  - Each block turns off tracing and allexport (`set +x +a`) before the read and clears the variable with `unset -n` then `unset -v`. If either fails, as it does for a readonly name, the block refuses with exit 2. The four reads that lacked `|| exit 2` now have it.
  - `code-server.md`: the session-cookie probe now rejects any control character in the cookie, not only a carriage return.
  - `gitops-controllers.md`: the Argo CD token probe now refuses an empty or control-character token. The Flux `generic-hmac` probe now runs in its own subshell and refuses an empty key; the subshell's exit discards the key and the signature, so the trailing `unset` is gone. The key still reaches `openssl` on its command line, as the block's comment says.
  - `haproxy.md`: the admin-password and stats-password probes now refuse an empty or control-character password. The admin probe escapes the password in place instead of copying it into a second variable, because the exception allows only one.
  - `surrealdb.md`: the system-user JWT probe, whose existing pattern check already refuses an empty or control-character token.
  - The six steps stay REASONED or UNVERIFIED, so no verification status changed. Row 1.126 stays open for the remaining sites.
- `CONTRIBUTING.md` (#296), carrying the maintainer's rulings of 2026-09-24.
  - Rule 5 now requires two things. A review brief for a demonstrated step states the exposed run, the fixed run and what each observed. Adding a demonstration run means rereading the guide's run-provenance sentence. Both lines were proposed by guardrails.
  - Rule 7 now allows one exception to its no-named-variable rule. `read` cannot target the positional parameters, so a key read with `read -r -s` may sit in a single subshell-local variable, if tracing and allexport are off (`set +x +a`), the variable is cleared first (`unset -n` then `unset -v`, failing closed), and it is never exported. A secret the reader already holds in an environment variable is not covered.
  - Row 1.126 tracks checking the 15 existing `read -s` invocations in nine guides against those guards.

- Follow-ups (#291) from #289's and #290's final reviews: `streamlit.md`'s realm sentence is punctuated so it parses on one reading, and `ray.md`'s Verify comment marks its whole `10001` expectation (the node's address plus a loopback listener) as read from source, citing the pinned Ray Client server, proxier and launcher lines in Sources.
- `ray.md` (#290): the network-isolation step now says not to rely on Ray's bind addresses. In Ray 2.58.0's source the core gRPC servers listen on every interface unless the node address is exactly `127.0.0.1`, `::1` or `localhost`, and Serve replicas open a gRPC server on `[::]`. In four loopback runs with `--node-ip-address=127.0.0.1` the GCS still listened on `*:6379` (cause not established), and a watcher stopped each run because the authoring host forbids wildcard binds. The Verify `ss` comment no longer expects `6379` on the private interface only. Row 2.40 records that a live demonstration needs an isolated network namespace.

DONE row 2.36 says the `hd` and email checks were removed together.
- `streamlit.md` (#288), closing backlog row 2.36: the Verify checks were demonstrated on loopback against Streamlit 1.64.0 with native `st.login` and behind Caddy v2.11.4 and oauth2-proxy v7.15.4, using Keycloak 26.7.4 as the OIDC provider, in the exposed and fixed states, and the guide records what was observed. The run found two things. First, section 2's install step needs Streamlit's `auth` extra: with Authlib alone, every browser login attempt got `Internal Server Error` for want of `httpx`. Second, a separately unprotected websocket location gave an anonymous client the app's output while the page returned `401`. It also shows that with static serving off, `/app/static/` returns the app's HTML with `200`, so a static probe is judged by its body. The external vantage and the wildcard bind remain REASONED (row 1.121). Row 1.120 records a non-blocking precision note from #287.
- `search-engines.md` and `sqlite-http-frontends.md` (#287): the same-host TLS reverse-proxy pattern was demonstrated on loopback with Caddy v2.11.4, in front of Meilisearch (keyed, and keyless as the exposed state, where the paired block printed `FAIL`), of Typesense, and of a Datasette with no authentication of its own (`basic_auth` per `caddy.md`, every tested route `401` anonymously and `200` with the credential, the backend still answering directly on loopback). Identity-aware proxies remain REASONED. Closes row 1.119: the paired block's limit sentence now rules out an alias on any word the block uses (reserved words included), any function shadowing a builtin or command it uses, and a `command_not_found_handle` with curl missing, and names `enable -n`, `hash -p`, DEBUG traps and pasting inside a function. The sqlite-web CSRF sentence is scoped to the insert route.
- `sqlite-http-frontends.md` (#286), closing backlog row 2.39: the Verify checks were demonstrated on loopback against Datasette 0.65.5 (with datasette-auth-passwords and datasette-write) and sqlite-web 0.8.1 from PyPI, in the exposed state as well as the fixed one except for the listener bind, and the guide records what was observed. The run found that sqlite-web 0.8.1's insert route accepts an authenticated write with no CSRF token and a foreign `Origin`, with a session cookie that sets no `SameSite`; the guide now says so and advises `--read-only` behind the proxy. The rest is row 1.118; row 1.119 records notes from #285.
- `search-engines.md` (#285), closing backlog row 2.38: the Verify checks were demonstrated on loopback against the Meilisearch v1.53.2 and Typesense 30.2 release binaries, in the exposed state as well as the fixed one except for the listener bind, and the guide records what was observed. The paired-request block now clears its own variables first: it had printed a success line without sending a request when the calling shell held a readonly `audit_role`. The run confirmed the guide's JOIN warning: a Typesense key scoped to one collection read a joined collection's field until a scoped key excluded it. Proxy, container, firewall, external-vantage and client-bundle checks remain REASONED, tracked as row 1.117.
- `vault.md` and `nomad-consul.md` follow-ups (#284), closing rows 1.115 and 1.116 and demonstrating part of 1.114. In `vault.md`, block 1's health request prints `exit` and `err`, the wrapping block's refusals point at the sixty-second control, and a loopback run adds two file audit devices with one request correlated in both, the batch-token and long-lease exposed halves, and an unintended second listener (which also opened its own cluster port). In `nomad-consul.md`, the `CONSUL_HTTP_TOKEN` instruction moves next to its command, the defaults source names `enable_debug`, and a tokenless `200` on `/v1/acl/token/self` is named as the sign of a default token. The `vault.md` changelog entry for #283 now says two minutes, as the guide does.
- `vault.md` (#283), closing backlog row 2.30: the Verify checks were demonstrated on loopback against Vault 2.1.1 running the guide's own section 3 configuration, in the exposed state as well as the fixed one wherever the loopback runs allowed it, and each step now records what was observed. Two corrections came from the run: an expired AppRole SecretID still logs in until AppRole's once-a-minute tidy removes it, so the expiry test now waits more than two minutes past the TTL; and a token made with `vault token create` under the root token is revoked with it, so the root-retirement procedure now says to log the admin in through an auth method. Service tokens are identified by the `hvs.` prefix, and the wrapping refusals are noted as a plain `permission denied`. What these runs do not show is row 1.114; rows 1.115 and 1.116 record review notes.

- Added a Default credential column to `exposure-index.md` (#277), closing backlog row 1.107. Each of the 150 rows now says what its cited guides state a fresh install ships with: a well-known account, an empty or placeholder password, no authentication, a secure default the guide states, "not stated" where the guides are silent, or "varies by service; see the guides" where one row covers services with different defaults. The column records the guides, not the vendors. `tools/check_exposure_index.py` now also holds the table to a strict grammar: the exact header and separator, then every line up to the blank line that ends the table in `| a | b | c | d |` form, every non-Port cell built only from a whitelist (printable ASCII text, single-backtick code, links with text and a simple target, and escaped pipes) and carrying a letter or digit, and a Port cell that is a list of ports or ranges from 1 to 65535 with no leading zeros. The page must open with a `# ` heading, and the header must appear once, with a blank line above it and no `<`, `$` or code fence anywhere above it, so the gate reads the table GitHub renders, and every link's text must show a letter or digit. A row that breaks the grammar fails the gate and maps nothing, and the old three-column header fails closed; the self-test grows to 123 cases.

## 2026-09-23

- Hardened the reasoned-row gate's parsing (#272). `tools/check_reasoned_rows.py` now splits guide and backlog text into lines on `\n` only, so a U+2028 or U+2029 inside a paragraph no longer fakes a heading, and its backlog filename match now also treats `+`, `~` and a non-ASCII letter, digit or other numeric character beside a basename as part of a longer filename, so `archive+redis.md`, `redis.md~` or a basename touching a non-ASCII letter no longer counts as tracking `redis.md`, while quotes of any kind, dashes, brackets and `*` around it still do. Underscore emphasis is still not credited, because without code-span parsing it cannot be told apart from underscores inside a filename. The shared `tools/_markdown.py` closes a fence only when spaces or tabs follow the closing run, so a non-breaking space no longer closes it (CommonMark). Regression cases 18 to 21 were added. The corpus result is unchanged (53 reasoned guides, 20 grandfathered, 0 new), and every gate that uses `tools/_markdown.py` gives identical output before and after. A fence opened inside a list item is still not closed at the item boundary, which remains a disclosed limit.
- Added the reasoned-row gate (#271): `tools/check_reasoned_rows.py` flags a guide whose Verify section marks a step "reasoned" but has no demonstration backlog row (a row naming the guide) in `TODO.md` or `DONE.md`, the tracking CONTRIBUTING rule 5 requires. Wired advisory into `tools/run_all_checks.sh` so it never fails the suite; the 20 pre-existing reasoned guides without a row are grandfathered in `tools/reasoned_row_baseline.txt`, and a `--strict` promotion to a blocking gate is left for Architect sign-off. Closes the recurrence path behind the #270 D1 finding.
