- Added `mcp-clients.md` (#270), a new client-side companion to `mcp-servers.md`, closing TODO 1.83 and covering the MCP 2026-07-28 client authorization obligations the server-operator guide defers, each verified at its primary source: authorization-response `iss` validation and mix-up defence (RFC 9207 section 2.4 requires simple string comparison and reject-on-mismatch, applied before the authorization code is redeemed per MCP Authorization, with the advertise/present handling table so the guide does not overclaim that every response lacking `iss` is rejected, plus RFC 9700 section 4.4 mix-up context); discovery-metadata issuer identity (RFC 8414 section 3.3, the metadata `issuer` must equal the issuer used to construct the well-known URL or the document must not be used, distinguished from RFC 9728 section 3.3, which validates `resource`); Client ID Metadata Documents (draft-ietf-oauth-client-id-metadata-document-00) preferred over deprecated Dynamic Client Registration, with MCP's requirement to specify an `application_type`; refresh-token confidentiality (OAuth 2.1 section 4.3) and `offline_access` request handling (OIDC Core section 11 prompt=consent condition); and OAuth-discovery SSRF (RFC 9728 section 7.7 SHOULD, raised to a MUST for server-deployed clients by MCP Security Best Practices). The Verify section runs the SSRF address-classification and cleartext-token checks and marks the client-behaviour checks reasoned, naming the absent client-and-authorization-server harness, per the CONTRIBUTING Verify rule. - Added a NativeAuthenticator password and signup-policy control to `jupyter.md` (#262), the follow-up deferred from #260, source-verified against the pinned `nativeauthenticator` 1.3.0 source: when a Hub uses NativeAuthenticator instead of `PAMAuthenticator` (`c.JupyterHub.authenticator_class = 'native'`) the host no longer supplies a password policy, so set `minimum_password_length` and `seconds_before_next_try` (both declared with a `.tag(default=...)` annotation that is traitlets metadata, not the trait default, so both effectively default to `0` -- no length floor and essentially no cooldown), `check_common_password` (default `False`, matched against the package's bundled 10,000-entry common-password list), and `allowed_failed_logins` (lockout disabled at the default `0`) explicitly, and keep `open_signup` at its default `False`. Documents that NativeAuthenticator's admin-approval step is separate from JupyterHub's `check_allowed` admission and that, under the section 5 `allow_all = False` plus populated `allowed_users` configuration, a not-yet-registered `allowed_users` or `admin_users` username is pre-authorized at signup, so a listed name can be claimed at the signup form by anyone who reaches it; passwords are stored as bcrypt hashes in the Hub database. A new REASONED Verify check (no Hub with NativeAuthenticator in the authoring environment) exercises the password, lockout, approval-hold, pre-authorization-exposure, and `enable_signup` 404 behaviours, with the JUPYTER-LIVE-1 demonstration-debt row extended. - Added the pinned-citation gate (#254): `tools/check_pinned_citations.py` flags mutable GitHub source citations (`raw.githubusercontent.com/<owner>/<repo>/main|master/...` and `github.com/<owner>/<repo>/blob/main|master/...`), matching only the ref segment so a `main` or `master` inside a file path (for example a pinned tag's `.../src/main/java/...`) is not flagged. A citation against a mutable branch cannot be re-verified: the file can change while the URL still resolves, so both this offline gate suite and the advisory lychee sweep stay green as the evidence drifts. The gate lands ADVISORY: it reports the current mutable citations across the root Markdown files (baseline 91) and fails only if the count rises above that baseline, so the count can never rise above today's baseline while the corpus-wide pinning sweep proceeds as a tracked backlog. Includes a self-test and is wired into `tools/run_all_checks.sh`.
- Deepened `rabbitmq.md` (RabbitMQ 4.3) with residual deployment-hardening controls (#261), source-verified against RabbitMQ 4.3.6 and Erlang/OTP 27 (directives checked against the pinned `rabbitmq-server` v4.3.6 launch script and the OTP distribution-over-TLS documentation): encrypt inter-node and CLI distribution traffic with mutually authenticated TLS, configured through `rabbitmq-env.conf` (`SERVER_ADDITIONAL_ERL_ARGS` and `RABBITMQ_CTL_ERL_ARGS` selecting `-proto_dist inet_tls -ssl_dist_optfile`) and a separate Erlang-term `inter_node_tls.config` with `verify_peer` in both directions rather than a `rabbitmq.conf` setting, with the honest notes that distribution certificates are checked against the Erlang node name, that a plaintext node and a TLS node cannot cluster during a rolling switch, and that distribution TLS does not remove the Erlang cookie, epmd, or firewall requirements from the existing distribution-port section; and restrict routing keys on shared topic exchanges with `rabbitmqctl set_topic_permissions` (broker records managed by the CLI, not entries in `rabbitmq.conf` or `advanced.config`, available since RabbitMQ 3.7), noting that topic authorization is default-allow until a topic permission is set, that the write pattern gates publishing while the read pattern gates the routing keys a binding may use, that consumers still read from their queues under ordinary permissions so topic authorization does not filter messages already sitting in a queue, and that clearing a topic permission returns the exchange to the unrestricted default rather than denying it. New deployment Verify checks 8 and 9 are REASONED (no RabbitMQ, Erlang, or multi-node cluster in the authoring environment) with the RABBITMQ-LIVE-1 demonstration-debt row extended. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations pin to RabbitMQ 4.3.6, Erlang/OTP 27, and the official RabbitMQ documentation.

- Deepened `jupyter.md` (Jupyter Server 2.18+ and JupyterHub 5+) with residual deployment-hardening controls (#260), source-verified against pinned Jupyter Server 2.18.0, JupyterHub 5.5.2, and jupyter_client 8.6.3: preserve the deployment path prefix through the reverse proxy (`c.ServerApp.base_url` for a standalone Server, `c.JupyterHub.bind_url` setting the listener and base URL together, with the note that the configurable-http-proxy `--no-include-prefix` and `--no-prepend-path` flags are not an authentication boundary); trust forwarded headers only behind a sanitizing ingress (a standalone Server's `ServerApp.trust_xheaders` defaults to `False` while JupyterHub enables forwarded-header trust, so bind the backend privately and set `trusted_downstream_ips`, which selects the client address but does not stop a peer from supplying headers and whose empty list does not disable trust); and encrypt internal Hub communication (`c.JupyterHub.internal_ssl`, default `False`, generating an internal certificate authority and per-component certificates with client-certificate verification across the Hub, proxy, and single-user connections, with the honest boundary that internal TLS does not encrypt the single-user-server-to-kernel ZeroMQ transport, whose HMAC authenticates but does not encrypt). New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no Jupyter runtime, Hub, or multi-user spawn in the authoring environment) with the JUPYTER-LIVE-1 demonstration-debt row extended. The deferred password-policy refinement is surfaced as a scope decision rather than folded in, because the guide targets PAMAuthenticator and the concrete controls belong to the separate NativeAuthenticator. - Deepened `mongodb.md` (self-managed MongoDB Community and Enterprise) with residual deployment-hardening controls (#259), source-verified against MongoDB 8.0 documentation (edition and version boundaries raw-checked against the pinned 8.0 pages): enable MongoDB Enterprise auditing with a deliberate destination and event coverage (`auditLog.destination`/`format`/`path` on every `mongod` and `mongos`, an `auditLog.filter` that narrows the recorded events, and `auditAuthorizationSuccess`, which defaults to `false` so successful authorization checks go unrecorded until it is enabled at a performance cost), noting the facility is Enterprise-only and the Community diagnostic log is not a substitute; redact document values from the diagnostic log (`security.redactClientLogData`, a MongoDB Enterprise feature, set through the `--redactClientLogData` flag or the configuration setting, replacing message-accompanying values with `###` while metadata stays visible and the documentation scopes it to the diagnostic log); encrypt the storage engine with a managed key (MongoDB Enterprise 3.2 native WiredTiger encryption, `security.enableEncryption` defaulting to `false`, a local `encryptionKeyFile` distinct from the cluster-authentication `security.keyFile`, or the recommended `security.kmip` key-server block, with the honest notes that MongoDB cannot encrypt existing data and the local-key procedure does not support rotation); encrypt selected fields with client-side field level encryption (CSFLE, which has no `mongod` switch, with explicit encryption available in Community while automatic encryption requires Enterprise or Atlas and automatic decryption stays in Community, supplying the encryption schema locally so a compromised server cannot drop the requirement, and the randomized-versus-deterministic equality-matching trade-off); and query encrypted fields with Queryable Encryption (equality queries generally available from MongoDB 7.0 and range from 8.0, requiring a new `encryptedFields` collection, unsupported on standalones, and never combined with CSFLE on one collection, with the same automatic-versus-explicit edition split). Every added control is edition-honest: auditing, redaction, and native storage encryption are Enterprise-only, CSFLE and Queryable Encryption split explicit-Community from automatic-Enterprise, and none is presented as a Community capability it is not. New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no MongoDB Enterprise deployment, encryption keys, or KMIP endpoint in the authoring environment) with the MONGODB-LIVE-1 demonstration-debt row extended. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations pin to MongoDB 8.0 documentation.

- Deepened `elasticsearch.md` (self-managed Elasticsearch 8.x and OpenSearch 3.x) with residual deployment-hardening controls (#258), source-verified against Elasticsearch/Kibana 8.19 and OpenSearch/OpenSearch Dashboards 3.8.0: field-level and document-level security (an Elasticsearch Platinum/Enterprise feature, since the free Basic security tier excludes FLS/DLS, versus OpenSearch Security's free Apache-2.0 equivalent, with each product's query and aggregation limitations disclosed and separate indices recommended where stronger isolation is required, and the two products' rules never cross-attributed); storage encryption as an operating-system and dm-crypt control independent of transport TLS (no native switch; Elastic's "Encryption at rest support" is a subscription row, not a feature toggle); snapshot repository encryption distinguishing server-side, customer-managed-key, and client-side encryption per product (noting OpenSearch removed the S3 `server_side_encryption` setting in 3.1 in favour of `server_side_encryption_type`, defaulting to `AES256` from 3.8, and not substituting Elasticsearch and OpenSearch settings for one another); and the separate Kibana and OpenSearch Dashboards browser-facing listener on 5601, which defaults to plaintext and needs its own TLS distinct from its connection to the cluster. New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no live cluster or eligible Elasticsearch subscription in the authoring environment) with the ES-OS-VERIFY-1 demonstration-debt row extended. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations pin to Elasticsearch 8.19 documentation and version-tagged OpenSearch references.

## 2026-09-20

- Deepened `redis.md` (upstream Redis) with HA-set hardening controls (#257), source-verified against Redis Open Source 8.10.1 (config directives raw-checked against the pinned `redis/redis/8.10.1` `redis.conf` and `sentinel.conf`): authenticate replication through role changes (`requirepass` plus `masterauth`, or a dedicated `masteruser`/ACL replication account, noting `masterauth` is outbound only and a replication account still receives the full stream); secure Sentinel's separate 26379 listener and its incoming and peer authentication (protected mode is off by default in sentinel mode; `requirepass`, or ACLs from Redis 6.2); give Sentinel dedicated per-monitored-instance credentials (`sentinel auth-user`/`auth-pass` with a restricted `__sentinel__:hello` channel ACL); restrict the cluster bus (the data port plus 10000, typically 16379; `cluster-port` and `cluster-announce-*` publish endpoints but are not a firewall); enable TLS independently for replication (`tls-replication`) and the cluster bus (`tls-cluster`), noting the guide's deliberate `tls-auth-clients no` also removes incoming replica certificate requirements while the cluster bus always requires a peer certificate; configure TLS on Sentinel itself and its outgoing connections; and restrict trusted HA peer identities with `tls-expected-peer-name` (new in Redis 8.10, so version-gated and not backported to the 6.0+ baseline, not enforced on Sentinel's outgoing path, and warn-and-continue on builds compiled without peer-name verification). New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no live Redis, Sentinel, or cluster in the authoring environment) with the REDIS-LIVE-1 demonstration-debt row extended to the seven HA checks. The Valkey compatibility note is kept separate from these upstream-Redis claims. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations pin to `redis/redis` 8.10.1 and the official Redis documentation.
- Deepened `minio.md` (AIStor) with residual deployment-hardening controls (#256), source-verified against the current AIStor documentation: disable the embedded Console when administration uses `mc`/API clients (`MINIO_BROWSER=off`, distinct from the `MINIO_BROWSER_REDIRECT` redirect control); set a workload-sized cluster-wide API concurrency budget (`MINIO_API_REQUESTS_MAX`, divided among nodes, neither a requests-per-second nor a TCP-connection limit); map OIDC identities to scoped policies (`MINIO_IDENTITY_OPENID_ROLE_POLICY` bound to the existing `app-bucket` policy, or the mutually-exclusive `MINIO_IDENTITY_OPENID_CLAIM_NAME` claim mapping (configuring both is a startup error), with the honest note that authentication is not authorization and that an explicit `AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity` `DurationSeconds` up to a year takes precedence over a short `MINIO_STS_DURATION` default and the JWT `exp`); and shorten presigned-URL lifetime (`mc share download --expire`, default 7 days) backed by a server-enforced `s3:signatureAge` deny policy on the non-root application identity. New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no live AIStor, IdP, or load client in the authoring environment) with the MINIO-LIVE demonstration-debt row extended. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use the official AIStor documentation.
- Pinned CI's shellcheck to 0.11.0 (#255) so the local gate and CI lint with the same version. `ubuntu-latest` floats shellcheck, and in #231 the runner's older shellcheck raised an SC2015 on a correct `[ -f ] && [ -r ] || { ...; exit; }` guard that the gate's local 0.11.0 does not, so a green local run reddened only in CI after push. The `Checks` workflow now installs a SHA-256-verified shellcheck 0.11.0 before running the suite, the same determinism reason its Python pin carries; the gate itself still reads `shellcheck` from PATH and stays offline, and a release-asset outage failing the job is the tradeoff already accepted for the Python pin. Updated the `check_shell_blocks.py` docstring to match.
- Deepened `php.md` (PHP and Laravel) with production-hardening controls (#253), source-verified against PHP 8.x and Laravel 12.x: harden php.ini error handling and the banner (`display_errors=Off`, `display_startup_errors=Off`, `log_errors=On` to a protected `error_log`, `error_reporting=E_ALL`, and `expose_php=Off` to drop the `X-Powered-By: PHP` header, starting from the bundled `php.ini-production`); reduce the code-execution, inclusion, and upload-directory surface (`disable_functions` as an application-tested deny list, `open_basedir`, `allow_url_fopen` where unneeded, `allow_url_include` kept Off, FPM `security.limit_extensions`, and never executing PHP from writable upload directories); bound uploads and requests (`upload_max_filesize`, `post_max_size` above it, `max_file_uploads`, `memory_limit`, `max_input_vars`, `max_execution_time`, `max_input_time`, and FPM `request_terminate_timeout`); and harden session storage and lifetime beyond the cookie flags (`session.gc_maxlifetime`, `session.cookie_lifetime`, `session.use_only_cookies`, `session.sid_length`/`sid_bits_per_character`, and `session.save_path` permissions). New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no PHP runtime in the authoring environment) with a PHP-VERIFY demonstration row (TODO 2.43); the local `php -i` directive inspection is likewise reasoned, since no PHP executable was available in the authoring environment. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use the official PHP manual and Laravel 12.x documentation.
- Deepened `dotnet.md` (ASP.NET Core / Kestrel) with server-hardening controls (#252), source-verified against .NET 10 LTS: keep the Developer Exception Page out of production (`UseExceptionHandler`, `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT` with Production as the unset default, the `DOTNET_` versus `ASPNETCORE_` precedence, and Development-only `AddDatabaseDeveloperPageExceptionFilter`/`UseMigrationsEndPoint`); bound request and connection budgets (`KestrelServerLimits.MaxRequestBodySize` 30 MB, `[RequestSizeLimit]`, `FormOptions.MultipartBodyLengthLimit` 128 MiB, the IIS `maxAllowedContentLength` distinction, plus `KeepAliveTimeout`, `RequestHeadersTimeout`, `MaxConcurrentConnections`, header count and size limits, minimum data rates, and the `Http2Limits` defaults, correcting stale window and field values); persist Data Protection keys for containers and replicas (`PersistKeysToFileSystem`, a shared `SetApplicationName`, `ProtectKeysWithCertificate`, and repository isolation, with the honest note that ephemeral keys break authentication and antiforgery rather than weakening the algorithm); and reduce auxiliary exposure (`AddServerHeader=false`, IIS `X-Powered-By`/server-header removal, Development-only OpenAPI/Swagger UI or `RequireAuthorization`, authorized health checks with the Host-header-spoofing caveat, and `AllowedHosts` host filtering). Also corrected the Kestrel `SslProtocols` default (None=OS, so the explicit TLS 1.2/1.3 is an intentional restriction), the lockout wording (`lockoutOnFailure: false` is the template's argument, not a parameter default), the forwarded-headers trust wording (loopback is trusted by default, and .NET 10 replaces `KnownNetworks` with `KnownIPNetworks`), the cookie `SameSite` claim (Strict is too categorical for the application cookie; correlation and nonce cookies default to None), the `SSL_CERT_FILE` (a file) versus `SSL_CERT_DIR` (a directory) conflation, and the Verify 401/403 expectation (pinned to a protected API and its scheme). New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no .NET runtime in the authoring environment) with a DOTNET-VERIFY demonstration row (TODO 2.42). Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use official Microsoft Learn, .NET runtime and ASP.NET Core source, IIS, and OpenSSL documentation.
- Deepened `java.md` (Spring Boot) with server-hardening controls (#251), source-verified against Spring Boot 3.5.16 and 4.1.1 (Spring Security 6.5/7.1): lock down Actuator (expose only `health` by default, bind the management listener privately with `management.server.port`/`management.server.address` or disable it with `port=-1`, authorize every endpoint with `EndpointRequest.toAnyEndpoint()` on a dedicated filter chain, with the warning that any custom `SecurityFilterChain` makes Boot's default web security back off so an Actuator-only chain leaves the rest of the app unprotected; keep `shutdown` and `heapdump` access `none` and `env`/`configprops` `show-values` `never`); keep production error responses closed (`server.error.*` in Boot 3 and `spring.web.error.*` in Boot 4, include-message/stacktrace/binding-errors `never` and include-exception `false`, noting on-param is not a boundary and disabling the whitelabel page alone is not a fix), keep the H2 console off (`spring.h2.console.enabled=false`) and exclude devtools from production; bound uploads, forms, and headers (`spring.servlet.multipart.max-file-size` 1MB and `max-request-size` 10MB, `server.tomcat.max-http-form-post-size` 2MB, `server.max-http-request-header-size` 8KB, none of which is a universal JSON body limit); and add proxy-forwarded-header trust, stateless-API CSRF, and session-fixation qualifications. Also corrected the "every endpoint requires authentication" overstatement (Actuator health is permitted, and a custom chain backs off Boot defaults), the HSTS claim (the default writer emits only when the request `isSecure()`), and the generated-password Verify check (zero log lines does not prove the default user is gone); added the Boot 4 moved-package citations (`ServerProperties`, `EndpointRequest`, `RestClientSsl`/`WebClientSsl`). New deployment Verify checks are REASONED (no Spring Boot runtime in the authoring environment) with a JAVA-VERIFY demonstration row (TODO 2.41). Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use official Spring Boot, Spring Security, Spring Framework, and Tomcat documentation.
- Deepened `go.md` (Go `net/http`) with server-hardening controls (#250), current to Go 1.27.1: keep diagnostic endpoints private (importing `net/http/pprof` or `expvar` registers `/debug/pprof/` and `/debug/vars` on `http.DefaultServeMux`, which a nil-handler server exposes, so give the app its own `http.NewServeMux()` and serve any diagnostics on a separate loopback listener); bound request resources (`http.MaxBytesReader` per endpoint with an explicit 413, plus the separate `Server.MaxHeaderBytes` and Go 1.27 `Server.MaxHeaderValueCount` header limits); set the full `http.Server` deadline set (`ReadTimeout`, `WriteTimeout`, and `IdleTimeout` beyond the existing `ReadHeaderTimeout`, on the port-80 redirect server too, with `http.NewResponseController` for per-request budgets); and set security headers in middleware when Go terminates its own TLS (`Strict-Transport-Security` only when `r.TLS != nil`). Also corrected the internal-CA snippet to check both errors and reject a false `AppendCertsFromPEM` (with the Go 1.27 `SSL_CERT_FILE`/`GODEBUG=x509sslcertoverrideplatform` note), softened the `InsecureSkipVerify` "every connection" wording (the `VerifyConnection`/`VerifyPeerCertificate` exception), and qualified privileged-port binding as Linux's configurable default (`net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start`). Extended Verify with local and guarded off-host pprof-reachability checks. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use pkg.go.dev and the Go project documentation, current to Go 1.27.1.
- Deepened `ray.md` with residual Tier-1 controls (#249), pinned to Ray 2.58.0 and KubeRay v1.7.0: treat `runtime_env` as code execution (a submitted job installs its `pip`/`conda`/`working_dir`/`py_modules`/`worker_process_setup_hook` before the entrypoint runs, and Ray ships no admission control or allowlist for it, so submitting a job or reaching the Client port is permission to run arbitrary code, mitigated operationally by prebuilding reviewed dependencies into the image); keep Ray Serve application endpoints private (HTTP proxy on `8000`, defaulting to `127.0.0.1` from the Python `HTTPOptions` but `0.0.0.0` from a config file, `proxy_location` default `EveryNode`, no built-in application authentication so cluster token auth does not gate Serve requests, and an optional gRPC proxy on `9000` only when configured); clarify GCS versus Redis on the head `6379` (it is the GCS metadata server, Redis stopped launching by default in Ray 1.11, so isolate the network and enable token authentication rather than setting a database password, and external Redis is only an opt-in GCS fault-tolerance backend whose `--redis-password`/`--redis-username` and KubeRay `gcsFaultToleranceOptions.redisPassword` authenticate the separate Redis connection and not GCS clients, with `RAY_REDIS_PASSWORD` not a head substitute for `--redis-password`); and run Ray processes with restricted privileges (the official image runs as `ray` UID 1000 but with passwordless sudo, so set an explicit non-root `securityContext` with dropped capabilities and a `RuntimeDefault` seccomp profile on the head and worker containers). Also corrected the token-authentication version and scope (still disabled by default as of Ray 2.58.0, introduced in 2.52.0, authenticating Ray's control plane and not Serve application requests), named `6379` as the GCS server, and qualified that the head and Client ports are unauthenticated only when token authentication is disabled. The new Serve and runtime_env material is guidance; the service-dependent Verify steps (external reachability, the token-authentication controls, and the gRPC-TLS check) are marked REASONED with a new RAY-LIVE-1 demonstration row (TODO 2.40), since the authoring environment has no Ray cluster; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to Ray 2.58.0 and KubeRay v1.7.0.
- Deepened `ollama.md` with Tier-1 controls (#248), pinned to Ollama v0.34.2: make the private listener explicit (`OLLAMA_HOST` loopback default versus a `0.0.0.0` or published-container exposure, and the official image's wildcard listener); put authentication, TLS, and MFA at the fronting layer, since the local API has no native authentication and serves plaintext HTTP, so an authenticating reverse proxy or tunnel is the security boundary (with the honest notes that `OLLAMA_AUTH` is a client-side outbound-signing variable, not an inbound switch, and that an `https://` `OLLAMA_HOST` prefix does not enable server TLS), and keep the shared-static-credential and no-constant-time-comparison limits of the bearer block explicit; reserve the management surface for administrators with a default-deny proxy policy that permits only required inference paths (excluding model pull/push/create/delete/copy, blob uploads, and account, experimental, and diagnostic routes); limit additional browser origins (`OLLAMA_ORIGINS` adds to the built-in origins rather than replacing them); run under a dedicated unprivileged account and protect the model store (`User=ollama`, a restrictive `UMask`, owner-only store); bound inference pressure and distinguish limits from defaults (`OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL` default 1, `OLLAMA_MAX_QUEUE` 512, `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` five minutes, VRAM-dependent context defaults, with proxy request-size and rate limits since these scheduler settings are not RAM, VRAM, or connection caps); control model provenance and registry transfers; disable unused cloud execution; and keep diagnostics private and disable request-body logging. Also corrected the `OLLAMA_AUTH`, CORS, and `https://`-prefix misconceptions, the parallelism-defaults-to-1 (not 4) and VRAM-dependent context wording, and the create-request file-handling description (validated relative filenames and digest paths at v0.34.2, not arbitrary host-file access); and replaced the mutable `main` Dockerfile citation with the v0.34.2-pinned reference. New service checks are REASONED with an OLLAMA-LIVE-1 demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to Ollama v0.34.2.
- Deepened `nats.md` (NATS and JetStream) with Tier-1 controls (#247): make credential ownership concrete (individually scoped password or NKey users, plus a conditional operator-mode JWT recipe with a resolver and preloaded system account); scope subjects with queue-group subscriptions and bounded `allow_responses`; isolate accounts with explicit stream export/import restrictions and a reserved `system_account`; bound JetStream at server and account level (`max_memory_store`/`max_file_store`, and per-account `max_memory`/`max_file`/`max_streams`/`max_consumers`/`max_bytes_required`/`max_ack_pending`) and separate JetStream administration (`$JS.API.>`) from message access; conditionally encrypt JetStream storage at rest (`encryption_key`, `cipher`); give cluster, gateway, and leafnode links their own authorization and TLS with peer verification and no `insecure`; keep the monitoring port private with the honest note that it has no native authentication; keep MQTT, WebSockets, and auth callout conditional and scoped (`allowed_connection_types`, an explicit browser-origin policy, an isolated callout account with an `xkey`); bound connections and slow consumers (`max_connections`/`max_subscriptions`/`max_control_line`/`max_pending`/`write_deadline`, and authorization and TLS `timeout`); and give each process only its required files (a non-root runtime and owner-readable secrets). Also corrected the pick-one-mechanism framing, the NKey generation step, the two successive authorization examples (resolved into one coherent configuration), the `deny`-overrides-`allow` qualification once a tracked `allow_responses` reply is permitted, the `verify_and_map` note, the outbound-only leafnode listener, the monitoring `/connz` metadata description, and the CLI Verify defects (the consumer-password pipeline that set a token instead of a password, the second terminal block that could not inherit the earlier subshell's server and credential context, and the missing `--inbox-prefix`); and replaced the two mutable `main` GitHub citations with a natscli v0.4.0-pinned reference and stable hardening documentation. New service checks are REASONED with a NATS-LIVE-1 demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to NATS Server v2.14.7 and natscli v0.4.0.
- Deepened `object-storage.md` (S3, Cloudflare R2, Google Cloud Storage, Azure Blob, and Supabase Storage) with Tier-1 controls (#246): keep new buckets private and remove the secondary permission system (S3 Block Public Access on all four settings at the account level plus Object Ownership "Bucket owner enforced", GCS uniform bucket-level access with public access prevention, Azure `allowBlobPublicAccess=false`, R2 with the `r2.dev` Public Development URL disabled, Supabase private buckets); scope access per principal and per prefix (S3 IAM roles and conditioned bucket policies, GCS IAM with the uniform-access requirement and 90-day irreversibility caveat, R2 least-privilege API tokens, per-operation Supabase RLS policies on `storage.objects` scoped with `bucket_id`); reject broadly privileged account keys (Azure disallow Shared Key, and no root, account-key, or service-role credential in client code); share objects only through short-lived signed URLs (presigned and SAS expiry bounds, with R2 presigned URLs valid on the S3 API domain and not on custom domains); enforce HTTPS-only transport and network-scoped access where supported; give application credentials a finite lifetime and a real revocation path; require encryption at rest with customer-managed keys where independent key control is needed (noting no encryption scheme repairs public permissions); and add object-lock/WORM retention and access or audit logging for irreplaceable data. Also corrected the GCS seven-day CLI-signing qualification (it also works with an activated service account, not only `--private-key-file`), the Azure SAS expiration-policy behavior (`Log` records and permits out-of-policy use while `Block` denies it) and the Shared Key wording (account-key-authorized requests are rejected, rather than account-key SAS being impossible to generate), the Azure anonymous-denial status codes (the remediation page documents `401`/`409` while the configuration page documents `403`, so no single code is universal), the R2 custom-domain qualification (a custom domain can be protected by Cloudflare Access or WAF, so "no custom domain" is one valid private baseline and not the only secure one), and the Supabase ownership example (add `bucket_id` for a bucket-specific policy). New service checks are REASONED with an OBJECT-STORAGE-LIVE demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations use official AWS, Cloudflare, Google Cloud, Microsoft Azure, and Supabase documentation.
- Deepened `firebase-supabase.md` (Firebase and Supabase) with Tier-1 controls (#245): Firebase - ownership rules for all three engines (Firestore, RTDB with the root-cascade warning, and Storage separately), authorize Callable and HTTP Functions and keep emulators unreachable, enforce App Check per product and restrict the public API key, and disable unused sign-in providers with email-enumeration protection. Supabase - close Data-API exposure (RLS plus grants and exposed schemas), the current publishable/secret key model versus JWT signing-key rotation, harden `SECURITY DEFINER` functions (`search_path`, `http`/`pg_net`, `vault.decrypted_secrets`), separate Storage and Realtime authorization, and constrain signups and anonymous auth. Also corrected the "only server-side gate is the rules layer" overstatement, the Firestore `rules_version`/recursive-wildcard semantics, the automatic-grants and "no policy means no access" qualifications, the view and `SECURITY DEFINER` overbroad claims, the Verify owner-write expectation, and the bundle-scan key guidance. New service checks are REASONED with a demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Citations use official Firebase, Supabase, and PostgreSQL documentation.
- Deepened `pocketbase.md` (PocketBase and Appwrite) with Tier-1 controls (#244): PocketBase - extend authorization beyond the five API rules to the auth `manageRule`, protected file fields with `viewRule` and file tokens, and realtime list/view subscription rules; operator protection (`superuserIPs`, proxy-restricted `/_/`) and explicit auth defaults (MFA/OTP for `_superusers`, `authRule`, the stateless-token caveat); settings encryption via `PB_ENCRYPTION_KEY` and `--encryptionEnv`; rate limits (`rateLimits.enabled`, default false) and working SMTP; and TLS/proxy-trust/`--origins`. Appwrite - collection/document permissions and `documentSecurity`, the server-key permission bypass and least-privilege key scopes; `_APP_CONSOLE_WHITELIST_*` registration and `_APP_OPTIONS_ROUTER_PROTECTION` hostname enforcement; `_APP_OPENSSL_KEY_V1`; `_APP_OPTIONS_ABUSE` and `_APP_OPTIONS_FORCE_HTTPS`; upload limits/antivirus and the function `execute`/scope boundary with a private executor/orchestrator. Also corrected the first-user bootstrap overstatement (the 30-minute installer token), the "no rule denies everything" overbroad claim (unprotected files; client-created grants), and replaced the two ineffective `curl -I` Verify checks with real authorization and registration probes. New service checks are REASONED with a demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to PocketBase v0.40.4 and Appwrite 2.2.0.
- Deepened `gradio.md` with four Tier-1 controls and a Gradio 6.28.0 currency update (#243): disable optional execution surfaces and distinguish API visibility from authorization (`GRADIO_VIBE_MODE` unset, `launch(mcp_server=False)`, and the 6.x `footer_links`/`api_visibility` replacements for the removed `show_api`/`api_name=False`); bound queued work and close the direct-API queue bypass (`queue(api_open=False, default_concurrency_limit, max_size)`, `launch(max_threads, max_file_size)`); restrict server-side URL fetching as an SSRF/outbound surface (no destination-allowlist argument, so deployment egress limits and `gr.load` target trust, with the 6.16.0/6.20.0 fix history); and disable unnecessary persistent collection (`flagging_mode='never'`, `run_history=False`, `analytics_enabled=False`). Also isolated the file cache (`GRADIO_TEMP_DIR`, allowed/blocked-path env inheritance), added external-auth/proxy/session guidance (`auth_dependency`, `server_name`, `root_path`, no server-side token expiry), and corrected the allowlist framing, the returned-path dotfile note, the upload-limit 6.20.0 baseline, and the port-search comment. New service checks are REASONED with a GRADIO-LIVE backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to the gradio@6.28.0 release tag.
- Deepened `open-webui.md` with six Tier-1 controls (#242): keep authentication on and persist a secret signing key (`WEBUI_AUTH`, an injected `WEBUI_SECRET_KEY`, `JWT_EXPIRES_IN`, Redis-backed revocation); close alternative registration and admin-impersonation paths (the `WEBUI_AUTH_TRUSTED_EMAIL_HEADER` proxy-strip requirement and the OAuth signup/domain/merge/role switches); restrict server-side Tools/Functions and code execution (`ENABLE_PLUGINS`, additive-across-groups workspace tool permissions, `ENABLE_CODE_EXECUTION`/`ENABLE_CODE_INTERPRETER`); constrain model access, backend connections, and admin data access (`BYPASS_MODEL_ACCESS_CONTROL`, private model access control, pinned connections, `ENABLE_DIRECT_CONNECTIONS`, `ENABLE_ADMIN_CHAT_ACCESS`/`ENABLE_ADMIN_EXPORT`); keep API keys off unless needed and endpoint-restricted (`ENABLE_API_KEYS`, `ENABLE_API_KEYS_ENDPOINT_RESTRICTIONS`); and bound retrieval, network, and resource abuse (`ENABLE_LOCAL_WEB_FETCH`, `AIOHTTP_CLIENT_ALLOW_REDIRECTS`, `RAG_FILE_MAX_SIZE`/`RAG_FILE_MAX_COUNT`, with the honest note that the upload and URL-ingestion handlers do not check the feature permission, so proxy request-body and route limits are also required). Also corrected the first-admin empty-database signup-bypass warning, the effective-signup-default lifecycle, and the `ENABLE_PASSWORD_AUTH`/trusted-header ordering. New service checks are REASONED with an OPENWEBUI-LIVE backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to commit 0a7c15832fb30b1903753e83f81dc7d27e5b0944.
- Deepened `mosquitto.md` with five Tier-1 controls (#241): topic authorization via `acl_file` (per-user `topic`/`pattern` read/write rules, `$SYS` isolation, and the subscribe-versus-message-access caveat) so authenticated devices cannot read or write each other's topics; explicit resource limits (`max_connections`, `max_packet_size`, `message_size_limit`, `max_inflight_messages`/`max_queued_messages`/`max_queued_bytes`, `max_keepalive`, with verified 2.0/2.1 defaults); per-listener scoping via `per_listener_settings` (and its 2.1 deprecation in favour of `listener_allow_anonymous`/`plugin_load`), including WebSocket listeners and the 2.0 explicit-listener default change; bridge security (`bridge_cafile`, `bridge_insecure false`, scoped remote credentials and topics, `try_private`); and host-side protection (privilege drop, persistence/log/secret file permissions, and the 2.0 early privilege drop). Also corrected the 2.0 anonymous-default note, the `mosquitto_passwd -D` reload/termination caveat, and the Verify CONNACK/exit-code guidance. New service checks are REASONED with a MOSQUITTO-LIVE backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Source citations are pinned to the v2.0.22 and v2.1.2 release tags.
- Deepened `litellm.md` with six Tier-1 controls (#240): per-key spend and throughput limits (`max_budget`/`budget_duration`, `rpm_limit`/`tpm_limit`, `max_parallel_requests`, with shared Redis required for correct limits and revocation across replicas, and `fail_closed_budget_enforcement` noted as not an absolute cap); explicit model-access allowlists on keys and teams (`models`, warning that `[]` means all models and that the master key bypasses model restrictions); finite key lifetime and real revocation (`duration`, `/key/delete`, `/key/block`, with in-place regeneration Enterprise-gated); separation of application credentials from admin authority plus removal of public surfaces (narrow `allowed_routes`, `admin_only_routes` exact-match and Enterprise-only, `NO_DOCS`/`NO_REDOC`/`NO_OPENAPI` with the URL-override caveat); constrained caller-controlled destinations and pass-through SSRF (`api_base` pinning is not an egress allowlist, `pass_through_endpoints` with `auth: true`); and keeping request content out of logs (`turn_off_message_logging`, `store_prompts_in_spend_logs: false`). Also corrected the pass-through-auth Enterprise claim (removed on current main), the `/metrics` and health-route auth caveats, the SSO five-user allowance, and the unsafe-master-key startup refusal. New service checks are REASONED with a LITELLM-LIVE backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. PII masking guardrails and prompt-injection detection are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `memcached.md` with four Tier-1 access controls (#239): run under a dedicated unprivileged account (`-u`, no root, no shared login); bound authenticated and unauthenticated pressure with a connection cap and an item-memory cap (`-c`, `-m`) so connection file descriptors and total item memory stay bounded (bounded budgets, not a request-rate or eviction guarantee); disable cache-wide invalidation and the remote shutdown command (`-F` for `flush_all`, no `-A`); and disable key enumeration and live watch access (`-X` to disable the `stats cachedump`/`stats detail` and `lru_crawler metadump`/`mgdump` surfaces, `-W` to refuse `watch`). Also hardened the network posture (private `-l` binding, `-U 0` to turn UDP off so memcached cannot be used as a reflection amplifier) and corrected the SASL notes (binary protocol only, and the patched-baseline version). New service checks are REASONED with a MEMCACHED-LIVE backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. TLS (`--enable-tls` builds), Unix-domain-socket exposure, and the remaining optional command surfaces are recorded as a follow-up.

## 2026-09-19

- Deepened `kafka.md` with four Tier-1 access controls (#238): separate client, replication, and controller listeners with per-listener security protocols and internal mTLS (KRaft roles, private bindings, `ssl.endpoint.identification.algorithm`); separate application roles and remove topic-creation privileges (per-principal Read/Write/Describe ACLs, `auto.create.topics.enable=false`, transactional-id ACLs); bound authenticated traffic and unauthenticated connection pressure (per-user quotas and connection limits); and retain authorization decisions (authorizer logging). Also applied the existing-guide corrections (AclAuthorizer vs the older SimpleAclAuthorizer, and the IdempotentWrite nuance). Preserves the existing client-side and Redpanda sections. New service checks are REASONED with a KAFKA-LIVE backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Certificate principal mapping, SASL session bounds after rotation, delegation-token lifetime, and JMX exposure are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `vault.md` with four Tier-1 access controls (#237): make application ACL scope concrete (a path-scoped KV v2 policy, no root/sudo for applications); bound application credentials and issued tokens separately (AppRole with short SecretID and token TTLs, use limits, bound CIDRs, and no default policy, with Kubernetes/JWT alternatives noted); require response wrapping for bootstrap-secret delivery (a `min_wrapping_ttl` policy plus `-wrap-ttl` and validated unwrap); and keep metrics and profiling authenticated (`unauthenticated_metrics_access`/`unauthenticated_pprof_access` off, a scoped `sys/metrics` collector identity). Preserves the existing seal-custody, audit-device, TLS, and storage sections. New service checks are REASONED with a VAULT-LIVE-1 backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Forwarded-address trust behind a proxy and authentication lockout are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `neo4j.md` with four Tier-1 authorization and resource controls (#236): replace the broad application role with a scoped Enterprise custom role using database, label, and property grants (`GRANT MATCH {props} ON GRAPH ... NODES ...`), while being honest that Community Edition has no role system; deny procedures, user-defined functions, and data loading to applications that need none (`DENY EXECUTE PROCEDURES/FUNCTIONS *`, `DENY LOAD ON ALL DATA`); bound transaction memory and set a default transaction timeout (with the client-override caveat); and retain security events and configure query logging without unnecessary secrets. Also applied the edition correction (Community supports multiple native users but every user has implied administrator privileges). New service checks are REASONED with a NEO4J-LIVE-1 backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Connector and advertised-address hardening, import-root and APOC-export controls, and mutual-TLS client trust are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `clickhouse.md` with four Tier-1 access and resource controls (#235): complete the SQL-driven administrator handoff and introduce table-scoped roles (a separate SQL admin, `sha256_hash` accounts restricted by `HOST IP`, and retiring the default account); lock query classes with a readonly settings profile plus memory and time limits and a per-user quota; make external-source restrictions concrete (`remote_url_allow_hosts`, withholding `URL`/`FILE`/`S3`/`REMOTE` source privileges); and configure and protect the query and session logs. Also fixed the existing Verify guard to the C2 case-arm shape and clarified the bootstrap administrator. New service checks are REASONED with a CLICKHOUSE-LIVE-1 backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Insecure auth-type prohibition, inter-node TLS/auth for clusters, and row-level security are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `mysql.md` with four Tier-1 account and file-access controls for MySQL and MariaDB (#234): scoped runtime accounts with host-qualified, named-table grants (dangerous privileges to withhold, roles as an alternative); removal of anonymous, unused remote-root, and test-schema access (`mysql_secure_installation` / `mariadb-secure-installation` with an inventory); disabling client `LOCAL` loading and restricting server file access (`local_infile`, `secure_file_priv`); and password validation and reuse policy (`validate_password` and MariaDB equivalents, expiration optional). Also applied the existing-guide corrections. New service checks are REASONED with a MYSQL-LIVE-1 backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Account locking/brute-force, error-log hygiene, client-certificate identity, socket-only operation, an edition-appropriate audit facility, per-account connection limits, and symlink support are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `postgresql.md` with four Tier-1 least-privilege and audit controls (#233): separate runtime privileges from object ownership (a `NOLOGIN` owner plus group-role grants and default privileges); restrict database `CONNECT` and public-schema `CREATE` (with the PostgreSQL 15 public-schema caveat); secure `search_path` and privileged-function execution (`SECURITY DEFINER` hardening); and connection plus DDL audit logging (`log_connections`, with pgaudit noted as an extension). Also applied the existing-guide corrections. New service checks are REASONED (read-only authoring environment, no writable PostgreSQL deployment) with a POSTGRESQL-LIVE-1 backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Authentication and connection time bounds, row-level security, client-certificate revocation, and automated-client credential storage are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `rabbitmq.md` with five isolation and exposure controls (#232): per-application vhosts with bounded resource permissions (dropping the broad `/` grant); management-plugin HTTPS on its own private listener; monitoring roles separated from administrator; concrete private AMQP and inter-node distribution bindings (no wildcard listener, epmd address pinned); and treating definitions imports as privileged provisioning input. Also applied the existing-guide corrections. New service checks are REASONED with a RABBITMQ-LIVE-1 backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Inter-node distribution TLS (including CLI clients) and routing-key restrictions on shared topic exchanges are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `jupyter.md` with five Tier-1 hardening controls (#231): run the execution service unprivileged (`allow_root` false, with terminals treated as an interface to remove, not a sandbox); preserve origin, XSRF, and DNS-rebinding/Host checks; give JupyterHub users individual identities and execution accounts (PAM admission, `LocalProcessSpawner`, never `SimpleLocalProcessSpawner`); isolate per-user browser origins and protect server configuration (`subdomain_host`, host-prefixed cookies, `disable_user_config`); and require authentication for otherwise unclassified endpoints. Also applied the existing-guide corrections. New service checks are REASONED with a JUPYTER-LIVE-1 backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Reverse-proxy/forwarded-header trust, password-policy refinement, and internal Hub/kernel transport encryption are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `mongodb.md` with least-privilege authorization and membership controls (#230): collection-scoped custom roles with explicit SCRAM-SHA-256 application credentials (password by prompt, not argv); internal authentication for replica-set and sharded-cluster members (x.509 membership or a shared keyfile, with `transitionToAuth` kept off); per-user `authenticationRestrictions` and `clusterIpSourceAllowlist` source pinning; and client x.509 identity mapping in `$external` kept separate from member certificates. Also corrected the end-of-life version guidance, the SCRAM-default nuance, the private-key creation umask window, the TLS config-merge/restart step, the mixed-mode TLS wording, and the Verify discrimination (`-g`, placeholder guard, and no listDatabases-as-authorization). New service checks are REASONED with a MONGODB-LIVE-1 backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Enterprise auditing, log redaction, storage encryption, and CSFLE/Queryable Encryption are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `redis.md` with single-instance ACL and runtime hardening (#229): make ACL restrictions durable and replace old permissions explicitly (`ACL SETUSER` with `reset`, persisting via `CONFIG REWRITE` or the ACL file, and active-session revocation with `CLIENT KILL`); separate read/write key permissions and Pub/Sub channel permissions; and lock down runtime configuration, debugging, modules, and scripting through ACL categories (preferring ACLs over the deprecated `rename-command`). Verify adds an ACL-failure review (`ACL GETUSER`/`ACL LOG`/`ACL DRYRUN`) with credentials supplied through `--askpass` and `REDISCLI_AUTH` rather than argv, marked REASONED with a backlog row. Regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Replication, Sentinel, cluster-bus, and TLS-peer authentication (the HA/cluster set) are recorded as a follow-up.
- Amended CONTRIBUTING rule 5 to codify an environment-capability carve-out for Verify posture (#229): a service counts as needing infrastructure the author does not have when no available, authorized environment can reproduce its exposed state, so a guide whose every Verify step is reasoned may ship under the existing impracticality allowance when each step is marked, backlog-tracked, and never presented as verified; the mere current absence of an obtainable runtime or binaries does not qualify, and the deprecated-project prong is unchanged. Also corrected an `undemonstrated` typo in `redis.md`.
- Deepened `elasticsearch.md` with three access-control and audit controls for both Elasticsearch and OpenSearch (#228): application RBAC with scoped, expiring API keys and least-privilege roles (native users and custom roles, the OpenSearch security API, with the paid LDAP realm marked as such); anonymous-access lockdown and narrow CORS; and security audit logging (Elasticsearch's is paid Platinum/Enterprise, OpenSearch's is free). Each control states its license tier and version caveats. Also corrected the existing Verify probes to lead with `-q -g` and to verify OpenSearch against its deployed production CA rather than the demo CA. New service checks are marked REASONED with a demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`. Field/document-level security, storage and snapshot encryption, the Kibana/OpenSearch-Dashboards listener, and remote-reindex allowlisting are recorded as a follow-up.
- Deepened `minio.md` with four AIStor data-protection controls (#227): scoped application access keys with least-privilege policies (`mc admin policy`/`mc admin accesskey`), server-side encryption via a MinIO KMS or KES key manager (documenting the `MINIO_KMS_AUTO_ENCRYPTION` default as active only when a key manager is configured, and the backend-encryption dependency), object locking and retention (GOVERNANCE vs COMPLIANCE, existing-bucket support on current releases, per-version retention and legal hold), and audit-log publishing to a webhook or Kafka target (none is configured by default). Replaced the Verify block's `mc alias set` credentials-in-argv with the `mc alias import` stdin form, broadened the title, and expanded the README and site descriptions. New service checks are marked REASONED with a demonstration backlog row; regenerated the plugin references and `site/llms-full.txt`.
- Updated the three Puma citations in `ruby.md` from the repository's old `master` branch to its canonical `main` branch (#226), a canonical-branch currency fix surfaced by the weekly advisory link sweep (the `master` URLs still redirect, but `main` is canonical). The sweep's other flagged citations are benign redirects (a trailing slash, learn.microsoft.com version parameters, or a documentation landing page) or bot-blocked pages that resolve normally in a browser, and are left unchanged.
- Fixed a false positive in `check_guard_conventions.py --strict-guards` (#225): the opt-in C2-WARN-ONLY-GUARD rule flagged a probe already covered by an effective C2 sentinel guard (`mosquitto.md:69`, whose `nc` sits in the guarded `*)` arm of a case sentinel), because it picked the first probe after a warn-only sentinel `if` without checking the probe's own coverage. The strict rule now suppresses a probe only when every operand variable it interpolates is covered by a case guard whose subject is that same variable (the main C2 coverage plus a subject-operand match, computed regardless of `--no-c2`; an unknown subject or operand denies suppression). It no longer inherits C2's unrelated-value blind spot: the mosquitto false positive is gone, while a probe using one variable under a case guarding another is still flagged. Main C2 emission is byte-unchanged. Self-test grows from 157 to 213. This resolves TODO row 3.14: the general guard-grammar auto-gate stays UNIMPLEMENTED (a zero-false-positive lexical gate for the if/elif, `-z`, flag-variable, and renamed-sentinel shapes is not justified, because there are no corpus true-positives to gain and the corpus guard-then-early-exit idiom false-positives a lexical rule), and `check_guard_conventions.py --strict-guards` remains the opt-in manual audit tool; this change only removes that manual tool's one false alarm.

- Redefined and enforced the `check_guard_conventions.py` C2 rule (`C2-PROBE-OUTSIDE-GUARD`) (#220). A placeholder-guard `case` whose every `REPLACE_WITH_` sentinel arm unconditionally exits or returns now extends its coverage past `esac` to the close of its own subshell or function scope, so the house exit-guard idiom reads as safe; warn-then-fall-through, a conditional or backgrounded exit, an exit inside an inner subshell or command substitution, a redefined `exit`/`return`, `;&`/`;;&` fallthrough, a quoted non-matching pattern, and a probe inside the sentinel arm's own body all stay flagged. Sentinel recognition now inspects every `|` alternative of a case pattern rather than only the last, closing a pre-existing blind spot that left the placeholder-first idiom (about 72 of 97 corpus guard arms) unchecked. Correcting the recognizer surfaced three previously-hidden probe-quality defects, now fixed by guarding their placeholders (the `mcp-servers.md` Origin-control probes guard the auth-header file; the `model-servers.md` `/invocations` probe guards the served-model name); and 44 standalone probes that carry no reader-substituted value (a fixed loopback, an illustrative example host, or an interactively-supplied credential) across 18 guides now carry a one-line `# guard-conventions: allow` waiver stating why each is safe. With the corpus at zero C2 findings, `--no-c2` is removed from `tools/run_all_checks.sh`, so C2 is enforced on `main`. Six rounds of tri-family adversarial QA then hardened the rule: a case earns coverage only from a genuine, effective, unquoted sentinel alternative that is not pre-empted by an earlier overlapping arm (closing the arm-order, quoted-sentinel, bracket-expression, and pinned-prefix false negatives), and a quoted `()` argument no longer drops its command; the module's KNOWN REMAINING BYPASSES section now discloses the residual lexer and recognition limits inherent to a shell tripwire (composite punctuation, function-keyword and case-bodied guards, DEBUG-trap/eval/source/enable -n, negated-bracket and time-prefix recognition gaps, quoted heredoc, comment continuation, and placeholder dataflow). The self-test grows from 91 to 157 cases, pinning the termination-certificate, scope, alternative-recognition, and effective-sentinel rules including the false-negative canaries.
- Fixed two pre-existing probe-quality gaps surfaced by the #217 QA (#219), both out of scope for that credential sweep. In `mlflow.md`, the authorization Verify block now takes the experiment id as a fourth guarded positional (`REPLACE_WITH_A_REAL_EXPERIMENT_ID`), so the two `experiments/get` probes refuse to run against the unsubstituted placeholder instead of returning a misleading status (CONTRIBUTING rule 6); the id is carried through the escaping `set --` lines and interpolated into both URLs as `$4`. In `caddy.md` and `traefik.md`, the under-limit body-size positive control is tightened from "must NOT be 413" to requiring the app's own normal response (a 2xx, or its own 404 or redirect), never 413, and treats a 401 (credentials) or 000 (transport) as voiding the control rather than passing it (rule 5). No behavior change to a correctly substituted run.
- Added an Argo Workflows section to `workflow-orchestrators.md` (#218; codex gpt-6-astra source-verified against the Argo release-3.7 documentation and current upstream source, claude cross-checked), taking the guide from five tools to six. Covers the argo-server exposure surface: `--port` 2746 with HTTPS and `--auth-mode=client` the default since v3.0 (client mode uses the caller's own Kubernetes bearer token and that identity's permissions); the dangerous pre-v3.0 `--auth-mode=server` default, where argo-server uses its OWN ServiceAccount for API requests so anyone who can reach it acts with that account's permissions without authenticating, and adding another mode does not remove the anonymous fallback while `server` stays enabled; `--auth-mode=sso` (v2.9+) with SSO RBAC (`sso.rbac.enabled`, v2.12+) mapping groups to narrowly scoped ServiceAccounts via `workflows.argoproj.io/rbac-rule` and `-rbac-rule-precedence`, without which authenticated users share the server identity; OAuth client credentials in Kubernetes Secrets; and keeping 2746 private behind an authenticating HTTPS proxy with MFA, NetworkPolicy, TLS left on (`--secure=false` disables it), and least-privilege server, SSO, and workflow-execution ServiceAccounts, since permission to submit workflows runs arbitrary containers. The Verify block gains a reasoned `/api/v1/workflows/{namespace}` discriminator (a server-mode anonymous 200 list is the finding; client/sso return 401 without a token or 403 for an authorization denial) with the bearer token fed to curl on stdin via `--header @-`, never argv. Updated the title, the `ss` port list (2746), the README index row, and the Sources; backlog row 2.34 extended to Argo. Ships reasoned (no Kubernetes runtime in the authoring environment).
- Added a `check_guard_conventions.py` rule (checks C3-USER-ARGV, C3-HEADER-ARGV, C3-BODY-ARGV, C3-URL-ARGV) that fails any fenced shell block placing a live credential in curl's argv (a `-u user:password`, a credential-bearing `-H` header, a secret in a `-d`/`--json` body, or a `user:pass@` or presigned-signature URL), and swept the corpus onto the two safe stdin idioms it recognizes: `--header @-` for header secrets and `--config -` (with two-layer backslash-then-quote escaping and a control-character reject) for basic auth, request bodies, and signed URLs (#217, VERSION 1.0.217; new CONTRIBUTING rule 7 states the convention). The rule integrates into the existing guard-conventions fence walk, reuses the `# guard-conventions: allow <reason>` waiver, and self-tests it. Ships reasoned; backlog row 2.35 tracks demonstrating them. - Hardened `workflow-orchestrators.md` against a two-family audit (#210; codex gpt-6-astra full-source FIX with 25 findings, about 13 HIGH, cited against Airflow 3.3.2/FAB 3.9.0, Temporal Server 1.28.1/UI 2.34.0, Flower 2.2.0, Prefect 3.x, and Dagster 1.13.23; claude-fable-5 the offline lens, convergent on the Verify-discrimination and hygiene findings). Pinned Sources to the checked versions.- Fixed a guarded-probe defect in `clickhouse.md` surfaced by the workflow-orchestrators audit's offline lens (#209): the Verify subshell copied the validated positional into a named `host=$1` and then probed `"$host"`, the same guard-bypass class CONTRIBUTING rule 6 exists to prevent (a caller's `readonly host` makes the assignment fail while the probe reads the inherited value, and `declare -l`/`-u`/`-i` would transform it), so the probe could silently target the wrong host. Removed the named copy and used the positional `$1` directly in all three probes (plaintext `8123`, the HTTPS matched-triple `8443`, and the native `clickhouse-client`). - Hardened `supabase-self-hosted.md` against a two-family audit (#208; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with fifteen findings, about seven HIGH, all cited against `self-hosted/v0.8.1`; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). - Hardened `surrealdb.md` against a two-family audit (#207; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with ten findings, about seven HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens, convergent on the missing positive control, the REPL-not-scriptable probe, the argv password, the absent capabilities surface, and the unnamed version). SurrealDB is a multi-model database over HTTP/WebSocket, so an open instance is read/write of every namespace and database. Confirmed sound and kept: the bootstrap `--user`/`--pass`/`SURREAL_*` flags and `--unauthenticated` granting root-`OWNER`-equivalent access, the `127.0.0.1:8000` modern default, record users with default-deny table permissions, `TYPE BEARER`, native TLS via `--web-crt`/`--web-key`, and the listener-inventory and `/health` framing. Fixed: the version boundary (since 2.0.0 authentication is enabled by default, `--auth` became `--unauthenticated`, and the CLI bind default moved from `0.0.0.0` to `127.0.0.1`; omitting `--user`/`--pass` does not disable authentication or rotate an existing root password); the root password is now supplied through the environment or a prompt rather than argv; a new Capabilities section (`--allow-all`/`--deny-all` default off, network denied, functions allowed, scripting and guests off, more-specific-wins with deny-over-allow; `http::*` and JWKS retrieval make outbound requests, so restrict destinations and apply [egress-metadata.md](egress-metadata.md) because authentication does not prevent SSRF); system users at every level are governed by RBAC and bypass table and field `PERMISSIONS`, so a namespace or database system user is not record-level isolation; `TYPE JWT` grants system-level sessions whereas `TYPE RECORD WITH JWT` is the record-level path, and JWT trust needs issuer and audience enforcement (native `AUDIENCE` is a 3.3 feature, so on 3.2.x enforce it in `AUTHENTICATE`); `/signup` and `/signin` are public entry points, so omit `SIGNUP` when self-registration is unwanted (the legacy `DEFINE SCOPE` is removed in 3.0); and MFA belongs at the identity provider or an access gateway. The Verify was reworked into a paired `INFO FOR DB;` probe (no credentials versus a valid system-user JWT supplied through `curl --header @-` on stdin, against the same namespace/database and endpoint) plus a guarded origin-reachability probe, replacing the non-discriminating REPL check and the unrunnable `curl -vI https://`. The Verify ships reasoned because the authoring environment has no SurrealDB runtime; backlog row 1.82 tracks the live demonstration. Added capabilities, permissions, DEFINE ACCESS, 2.0.0-release, and HTTP-protocol references to Sources.
- Hardened `nats.md` against a two-family audit (#206; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with twenty findings, about eleven HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens, convergent on the credential-inheritance and cert-presenting negative controls, the unguarded placeholders, the argv password, and the missing route surfaces, and adding a plaintext no-credential probe). NATS and JetStream; an open server is publish/subscribe to every subject plus JetStream, KV, and object-store access. Confirmed sound and kept: the bare-server-unauthenticated-on-4222 default, `authorization` users/password/nkey/token, the `no_auth_user` warning, the allow-map syntax with deny-over-allow, the `verify`/`verify_and_map` TLS fields (both default false for client TLS), and the monitoring framing (8222 conventional, `/varz`/`/connz`/`/routez`/`/jsz`, no native login). Fixed: the cluster (6222), leafnode (7422), and gateway (7222) route listeners are separate surfaces with their own authentication and TLS (each defaulting its host to 0.0.0.0) that the client `authorization` and top-level `tls` do not cover, now a dedicated section; accounts and multitenancy (the shared `$G` account is not tenant isolation, so use distinct `accounts` with reviewed exports/imports) and the system account (`system_account`, default `$SYS`, whose credentials are administrative); the JWT signing hierarchy (the operator signs account JWTs and each account signs its user JWTs, not both by the operator); that `publish.allow` and `subscribe.allow` are independent; `default_permissions` and account scope for a user with no `permissions` block; a service-specific `_INBOX` prefix instead of `_INBOX.>`; private monitoring binding (`http: "127.0.0.1:8222"`, and `https_port` does not authenticate); and `max_connections`/`max_payload` (defaults 65536 and 1 MiB) plus a non-root process identity. The Verify was reworked: matched authenticated publish and subscribe positive controls with the password via the `NATS_PASSWORD` environment variable rather than argv, negative controls in a CLEAN environment (`env -i` plus `--no-context`, because an empty `--context` name does not clear the `NATS_*` variables) including a plaintext no-TLS no-credential probe whose success is itself the exposure, a forbidden-subscribe test that requires a permissions error rather than a quiet timeout, and a guarded monitoring probe; the unverified verbatim `:8222` documentation quote was paraphrased. The Verify ships reasoned because the authoring environment has no NATS runtime; backlog row 1.81 tracks the live demonstration. - Hardened `haproxy.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#205; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with sixteen findings, six HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens, convergent on the body-size false-pass, the unmatched 401, and the missing management plane). HAProxy 3.0 TLS terminator and reverse proxy; its frontend, stats interface, runtime API, and directly reachable backends are separate exposure surfaces. Confirmed sound and kept: `bind ssl crt` with `ssl-min-ver TLSv1.2` and the HTTPS redirect, the crypt(3) `$6$` hashed userlist and avoiding `insecure-password`, the `maxconn` global/frontend distinction and the advertised-length `req.body_size` guard with its chunked-upload limitation, and the `ss`-is-inventory plus direct-backend-bypass framing. The Verify was reworked into a guarded subshell: an auth triple (no, wrong, and valid credentials against one resource, the password supplied through `curl --config -` stdin rather than argv), the body-size cases run with valid credentials so a 401 cannot masquerade as the result plus the HAProxy-attribution fixture, payloads in a `mktemp -d` with a cleanup trap, `-q -g`/`-sS`/`--noproxy`/timeouts, and reasoned stats, runtime-socket, and mTLS checks. The Verify ships reasoned because the authoring environment has no HAProxy runtime; backlog row 1.80 tracks the live demonstration. Repointed the Sources to the 3.0 Configuration Manual and Management Guide sections and added the Authelia integration page. (Per the arbiter, the brief's claim that HAProxy backend certificate verification defaults to `none` was not adopted; the guide makes no backend-default claim.)

The Verify gained a runnable authenticated positive control (the token read without echo and passed via stdin) paired with the anonymous probe and an origin test, `--connect-timeout`/`--max-time`/`-S` on the probe, a concrete `generic-hmac` webhook paired test (`X-Signature: sha256=<hmac>`), and tighter `.status.webhookPath` non-disclosure. - Hardened `connection-poolers.md` against a two-family audit (#203; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with thirteen findings, six HIGH; claude-opus-4-8, the offline lens, returned SHIP and diverged: it is network-blocked and could not source-verify, so its judgment was set aside for the source-verifying arbiter, whose Verify defects were reproduced and confirmed against the live guide). Covers PgBouncer 1.25.2 and pgpool-II 4.7.2. Confirmed sound and kept: the port and default inventory (PgBouncer 6432, client TLS `disable`, server TLS `prefer`, `auth_type=md5`, empty admin/stats lists; pgpool-II 9999/9898, `enable_pool_hba` false), the four-CVE 1.25.2 warning, the `auth_type=any` console analysis, the forced-`user=` and pg_hba-moves-to-the-pooler reasoning, and the `ss`-is-inventory framing. Fixed: the PgBouncer SCRAM pass-through prerequisites (client SCRAM, no forced user, identical secrets, and that local `peer` cannot supply the SCRAM exchange); `auth_query` activation and safety (`auth_user`, a trusted `search_path`, execution revoked from `PUBLIC`, and the CVE-2025-2291 `VALID UNTIL` check a custom query must keep); the pgpool-II backend-TLS fallback pinned to 4.7.2's `pool_ssl_negotiate_clientserver` (plaintext fallback is a limitation, not mandatory TLS, and a backend HBA rejection does not prove the pooler refused the downgrade); the `.pgpoolkey` mode-`600` requirement with `pg_enc` prompt-not-argument provisioning; and PCP credential provisioning (`pcp.conf` `username:MD5` via `pg_md5 -p`). The Verify was reworked: every probe is self-contained with TLS written literally in each connection string (the shared `$TLS` could inherit empty into a separately pasted block and silently drop to libpq `prefer`); the `RELOAD` runs under `ON_ERROR_STOP` in a guarded subshell (a following `grep` had masked a failed reload), with a caveat that a successful reload does not prove every HBA line loaded; a direct-database-bypass test requires the database to reject a client that must go through the pooler (`hostaddr` for the address, `host` for the certificate name); the PCP admin channel is tested with a correct and a wrong password on loopback-only 9898; and pgpool-II gets its own `PGPOOL SHOW enable_pool_hba`/`ssl` and `pool_hba.conf` inspection. The backend-certificate matched-control tests ship reasoned (no isolated pooler deployment in the authoring environment); backlog row 1.79 tracks them. Added the pinned pgpool-II 4.7.2, PgBouncer HBA-parser, AES-password, and PCP-options references to Sources.
- Hardened `code-server.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#202; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with ten findings, four HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens, convergent). code-server is browser-hosted VS Code, so an exposed instance is remote code execution and host filesystem access as the running user. Confirmed sound and kept: the SSH-forwarding-first preference, the `127.0.0.1:8080` bind and `password` auth default with a randomized generated password, the login rate limit (two per minute plus twelve per hour), and the single-factor login routed to a fronting MFA layer. Fixed: the Verify block did not discriminate authentication (a `-I`/HEAD request cannot tell a login page from the editor, and a rejection with no authenticated positive control proves nothing because a fronting proxy can answer 401 while the origin stays open), so it is reworked into a guarded subshell that keeps the response body, probes the same editor URL unauthenticated and then with a valid session (the cookie via a stdin header, never argv), probes every public origin address directly (any HTTP response there is itself a finding), and is confirmed in a browser; credential precedence is now explicit (`PASSWORD` and `HASHED_PASSWORD` override the file, the hashed form wins over plaintext, there is no `--password` command-line flag, and any flag overrides the file, with the path from `--config`/`CODE_SERVER_CONFIG`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`); the config example no longer drops the generated password when copied over the generated file and no longer ships an active certificate path (`cert: false` is the default and a bare `--cert` self-signs); the built-in proxy exposure surface is documented (`/proxy/<port>/`, `/absproxy/<port>/`, and `--proxy-domain` subdomains use code-server's own authentication that `auth: none` disables, plus `--skip-auth-preflight` and `--disable-proxy`) with an egress boundary linking [egress-metadata.md](egress-metadata.md); and the SSH forward now binds its local listener to loopback. Added the code-server FAQ and version-pinned references to Sources. The reworked Verify ships reasoned because the authoring environment has no running code-server; backlog row 1.78 tracks the live demonstration.
- Hardened `clickhouse.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#201; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with fifteen findings, four HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). ClickHouse is a columnar OLAP database, so an open instance is read/write of every table plus host and network pivoting through table functions. Confirmed sound and kept: the upstream port and default inventory (a `default` user with an empty password, `networks` `::/0`, and `access_management`; `http_port` 8123, `tcp_port` 9000, `mysql_port` 9004, `postgresql_port` 9005, `interserver_http_port` 9009 enabled, with 8443/9440/9010 shown commented), `password_sha256_hex`/bcrypt (72-character limit)/`GRANT`/`ssl_certificate CN`, and native TLS via `openSSL`. Fixed: the external-source and SSRF surface was missing (the `url()` table function and HTTP dictionaries fetch URLs, `file()` reads local files vs `remote()` reaching other servers, the `SOURCES` grants split into READ and WRITE forms since 25.7, and `remote_url_allow_hosts` is matched before DNS resolution and on every redirect while omitting the section allows all hosts) so the guide now covers it and links [egress-metadata.md](egress-metadata.md); replication has its own `interserver_http_credentials` that a SQL-user password and TLS do not provide (keep `allow_empty` false); the listed client listeners follow `listen_host` but replica traffic follows `interserver_listen_host`; the upstream base defaults are not universal (the official Docker image restricts the `default` user's network access when no `CLICKHOUSE_*` variable is set); `config.d`/`users.d` merge semantics matter (commenting an element out does not remove an inherited listener, so use `remove="remove"` and `networks replace="replace"`); and ClickHouse 26.2 introduced native TOTP for XML-defined users (`time_based_one_time_password`), which is not available for SQL-driven users. The Verify block was reworked into a guarded subshell that probes the SAME origin with `--noproxy '*'`, timeouts, and CA trust (never `-k`): the disabled plaintext port 8123 must fail (any HTTP status is a failure), an empty/wrong/correct credential triple runs as `app` with the key passed through stdin headers (`X-ClickHouse-User`/`X-ClickHouse-Key`, not argv), and native 9440 is tested as a separate authenticator; `ss` is reframed as a listener inventory. The example `listen_host` used a TEST-NET-3 documentation address and is now a private-IP placeholder. Repointed the server-settings Source off its index and added `remote_url_allow_hosts`, `GRANT`/`SOURCES`, table-function, native-TOTP, 26.2-release, configuration-files, Docker default-user, and client references. The reworked Verify ships reasoned because the authoring environment has no ClickHouse runtime; backlog row 1.77 tracks the live demonstration.
- Hardened `llm-observability.md` against a two-family, source-verified audit (#200; codex gpt-6-astra full-source returned FIX with seventeen findings F01 through F17, six HIGH; claude-opus-4-8 the offline lens). LLM tracing and observability tools (Langfuse, Arize Phoenix, Helicone, OpenTelemetry Collector) that store full prompts, completions, and provider API keys. The Langfuse forged-cookie analysis (`NEXTAUTH_SECRET`/`SALT`/`ENCRYPTION_KEY`), the Phoenix admin-password first-startup inertia, and the guarded Phoenix probe were confirmed sound; the findings were per-tool auth/exposure gaps and Verify discrimination. Phoenix: `PHOENIX_OAUTH2_<IDP>_ALLOW_SIGN_UP` defaults `True` so an OAuth provider self-provisions accounts, and local password login bypasses the IdP's MFA, so set it `False` and `PHOENIX_DISABLE_BASIC_AUTH=True` (F01); Phoenix also binds HTTP `0.0.0.0:6006` and a separate gRPC `[::]:4317`, and its Compose publishes PostgreSQL (F12). Helicone: it self-hosts with Better Auth whose `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` ships a placeholder default (`change-me-in-production` in the all-in-one image, `your-secret-key` in the repo Compose) that must be replaced, and its Compose publishes the backing stores (PostgreSQL, ClickHouse, MinIO/S3, Redis, MailHog) on host ports independent of the dashboard login, so the section now names Better Auth, replaces the secret, restricts signup, and removes or loopback-scopes every backing publication and the Jawn/S3 services (F02, F03).
